# Tools — the reasoning

Governs `lib/tool*.js` and `app/(tools)/**`. **Read this before editing there** — CLAUDE.md carries only the rules that bind code outside this area; the derivation, the evidence and the alternatives weighed are here.

The index row named only `lib/tool*.js` until #336, because `offline/notes-index.mjs` fails a glob that matches nothing and there was no `app/tools/` for one to match. #336 created the directory and widened the row in the same commit.

`lib/airtable/tool*.js` is additionally governed by `airtable-access.md` and `naming.md`, as all of `lib/airtable/**` is.

## What this track is (#334)

The company buys drills, grinders and the like, hands them out to sites, and gets them back when a project ends. None of that is recorded anywhere today — the only account of where a tool is, is whoever remembers carrying it. Each physical tool gets a QR label; a scan on a phone moves it between `In stock` and `Out`, and the app can then answer what is where.

**It is not a second product.** Same app, same domain, same Airtable base, same login, same `Users` and `Jobs` as everything on the materials axis. One difference, and it is a real one: a scan happens on a phone, on site, possibly by someone wearing gloves, so the tools screens assume a mobile width while every screen above them assumes a desk (#336).

**IT DOES NOT PASS THROUGH THE OFFICE, AND THAT IS THE FACT MOST OF THIS SCHEMA FOLLOWS FROM.** A site person buys the tool, registers it, keeps it and scans it. There is no purchase request, no approval chain and no invoice; nothing here is Admin-gated, and everyone who scans needs an account only because the scan records who performed it (#337). The materials axis is the opposite shape — an order the office places and reconciles — which is why almost nothing is shared between the two beyond people and jobs.

## Where the screens stand, and the one place a width is decided (#336)

`app/(tools)/layout.js` wraps the whole axis; `app/(tools)/tools/page.js` is the first screen on it. **Both stood under `app/tools/` until #348** put the axis in a route group, which moved the files and moved neither URL.

**NOTHING ABOVE THE TOOLS TRACK MOVED, BECAUSE THERE WAS NOTHING TO MOVE.** The screens on the materials axis share no layout file at all — `app/layout.js` was the only one in the tree — and each declares its own container inside its own page instead: eighteen of those twenty-one pages carried an `mx-auto w-full max-w-* p-8`, twenty-two declarations in all, four pages carrying two. **Counted again at #460, it is twenty pages of twenty-four and twenty-four declarations**, four pages still carrying two, and `/` and the three sign-in screens carrying none. So "the layout the materials screens share" was a repeated habit rather than a file, and giving the tools screens one of their own displaced nothing and needed no rearrangement of the axis above.

**WHAT THE TOOLS AXIS DOES DIFFERENTLY IS HOLD THAT CONTAINER ONCE.** A width settled above lands in as many places as there are pages, which is why #258 has to visit each of them; a width settled here lands in one file. That is the single structural claim this issue makes, and the layout's own header is where a reader editing a tools page meets it — a page that declares a width of its own puts the axis back to two containers, and nothing fails when it does.

**THE CONTAINER IS EMPTY, AND THE EMPTINESS IS THE POINT RATHER THAN AN UNFINISHED EDGE.** #336 decides where a width is decided and decides no value: no width, no padding, no color, no type. The reason is `docs/briefs/_shared.md`'s opening claim — nothing about this app's appearance was designed, and there is no version of it to preserve — so a value chosen here to make the first screen look finished would become the baseline a design has to justify departing from, which is the one outcome the whole design milestone is arranged to avoid. **This said #258 fills it, and it is the issues that apply the design's values to this axis** — from the names #462 declares, with #258 left to gather the screens above it (`The design's values on this axis`). The visible consequence was recorded rather than smoothed over: the first tools screen rendered with no styling at all, at a phone width and at a monitor width both. **#460 put the rail in it** (`The navigation`), and a screen's own width, padding and type are still no business of the layout's.

**THE ROOT LAYOUT GAVE NOTHING UP.** Everything it holds is needed by both axes — the document shell, the two font variables, `globals.css` with its `color-scheme: light`, and the `title` template that lets every page state only its own name. **And the one thing that would have had to come down was not there:** this app had no navigation shell, so there was no nav to split into a desk shape and a phone shape. The links on `/` are that page's own content, added one at a time by the issue that added the screen behind each, and #336's is the fifth by the same precedent. **#460 gave the tools axis the design's navigation**, in this axis's layout and no other until #258 (`The navigation`).

**THE SHORTEST ADDRESS ON THE AXIS BELONGS TO WHATEVER A LABEL CARRIES, AND THE QR IS WHY.** How many characters that string has decides the symbol's version, and each version step adds four modules to every side — so at the size a sticker is printed, a longer address is a symbol whose modules are thinner, and thinner modules are a worse scan in a gloved hand on a site. #336 read that as "the tool item's page takes the flat slot under `/tools`", and #339 and #340 were built on it; **#348 separated the printed address from the screen, and `The four addresses, and the one a label prints` is where that goes.** What survives from here is the premise rather than the arrangement: the printed address is a budget, and the screen behind it is not.

**THE FIRST SCREEN IS ONE HEADING, AND IT READS NO TOOL TABLE.** `getAllTools` names `/tools` in its own docstring as the list of kinds, and #339 is what puts that list there; a list invented ahead of it would be a shape that issue has to argue its way out of. So the page carries the heading its table's name gives it and nothing else, and the one operation it makes is the session's user read. What it does prove is the thing #334 and #335 could not: a tools screen renders, at both widths, inside a layout of its own.

**WHO REACHES IT IS #337'S DECISION APPLIED HERE, NOT A SECOND ONE.** `requireUser()`, which is every signed-in user with no Role and no Job scoping — that issue's body states it as a fact about the axis, and a page has to name a reader before its brief can. What stays with #337 is how the person holding the phone arrives and the signal for a scan on a job the scanner is not assigned to.

**THE CHANGE OF PREMISE IS RECORDED IN `docs/briefs/_shared.md` RATHER THAN HERE**, as a fifth entry in its constraints. That file is the design work's only input and it runs in a tool that does not read this repository, so a premise recorded only here would have to be inferred from a screen brief nobody has written yet. The half that belongs in this file is the derivation above; the half that constrains a drawing — that breakpoints, touch targets and a spacing scale are being chosen with a phone case beside them rather than widened into one afterwards — belongs where the design meets it.

## The first write screen: one form, two writes, no rollback (#338)

`/tools/new` takes a tool's name and a quantity and produces one `Tools` row, that many `Tool Items` rows, and a `Created` row in `Tool Log` for each of them — `Registered` until #455. It is the first screen on this base that creates a `Tools` row, and #339 and #340 had nothing to render before it. **It is a dialog over `/tools` and a tool's own page since #456**, and `The registration as a dialog` below is where that went.

**THE FORM IS ONE FORM, AND THE FIND-OR-CREATE IS WHAT MAKES THAT POSSIBLE.** Naming a tool the company already owns is the ordinary case rather than an error — buying six more of a drill is the same act as buying the first six — so there is no "new tool" path beside an "add to existing tool" path. Two forms would be two write paths for one rule, which is the duplication CLAUDE.md's own section is about, and the person filling one in would have to know which of the two they were in before they knew whether the name existed.

**WHAT THE ONE FORM OWES THEM INSTEAD IS THE MATCH, STATED BEFORE THEY SUBMIT**, and it costs no query: the page loads the whole `Tools` table in one operation — `getAllTools`, the `Vendors` shape, bounded by what the company has bought — and `lib/toolRegistration.js:matchExistingTool` applies the same key in the browser. That is `lib/prItemMerge.js`'s arrangement exactly: one pure module holds the key, the action applies it, the form previews it. **The preview is not the verdict and the module's own header says so** — the list is as old as the page, so the write asks Airtable again through `getToolByName`. **Since #456 that page is `/tools`**, the one opener with a name to type, and the list it loads is the one it lists.

**THE DUPLICATE GATE IS `upsertMaterial`'S SHAPE DOWN TO THE LOCK KEY, AND FOLLOWING IT WAS THE WHOLE DECISION.** Both tables carry a human-typed natural key Airtable cannot make unique. So: normalize inside the writer so no caller can create an unnormalized row, compare case-insensitively at the lookup, lock on the normalized lowercased key so names Airtable would call equal serialize against each other, and **do not update the name on a match** — the first spelling wins, for `upsertMaterial`'s own reason that rewriting it lets one person's capitalization overwrite another's under everything already linked to the row. The residual is `withKeyLock`'s standing one: two concurrent Vercel invocations can both read "nothing exists yet". Unlike the tool item family, the repair here is cheap — `Tools` mints no id, so a duplicate row merges by hand with no printed label going wrong.

**`/tools/new` IS SAFE BESIDE THE DYNAMIC SEGMENT AND THE ARGUMENT IS THE ID FORMAT RATHER THAN THE FRAMEWORK'S RULE.** Next.js matches a static segment ahead of a dynamic one, measured rather than read off the documentation: with a temporary page at `app/tools/[toolItemId]/page.js` alongside the form, `/tools/new` rendered the form and `/tools/HYE-TL-260909-001` rendered the dynamic page. **But precedence is not what makes this settled** — the id that slot holds could never equal a static segment this app would add. **It holds a `Tools` record id since #348** rather than a `Tool Item ID`, which strengthens the same argument instead of changing it: Airtable mints those as `rec` and fourteen characters. **#456 took the static route away**, so the dynamic segment takes `new` now and answers with the tool page's not-found; `offline/tool-routes.mjs` keeps any string under `app/` from linking there.

**A TOOL'S NAME NEVER APPEARS IN A PATH, AND THE CONCLUSION OUTLIVED THE REASON THIS PARAGRAPH GAVE FOR IT.** The reason was that the flat slot under `/tools` belonged to the tool item, so nothing else could have it. #348 moved the tool item off that slot and the conclusion did not move with it: a tool's name is human-typed and could be any string, so `/tools/[toolName]` is unavailable on its own terms — a name is not URL-safe and a rename would move the address. That is also what stopped a tool somebody names `new` from colliding with this form's route, which #456 took away. The slot holds the tool's RECORD ID now.

**THE TWO WRITES RUN IN SEQUENCE AND ARE NEVER NESTED, WHICH THE CONCURRENCY RULE DECIDES RATHER THAN A JUDGMENT ABOUT DAMAGE.** `createToolItems` holds the day-prefix lock across its whole batch; `createToolLogEntry` takes a per-tool-item lock of its own through `generateChildId`. Writing each log row inside the batch would bound the damage to one unlogged tool item and would nest two `withKeyLock` calls — which CLAUDE.md forbids and `lib/materialsCache.js` is the precedent against. So the log pass runs after the batch, and a failure in it can leave several tool items without a `Created` row. **#470 took the lock off the log pass** — a new tool item's first row reads nothing — so the pass stays after the batch by choice rather than by the rule: `A registration in batches (#470)`.

**WHAT AN UNLOGGED TOOL ITEM IS, AND WHY IT IS NOT REPAIRED.** The row is complete and usable — it has its printed id, `In stock`, and a job — and what is missing is the moment it came into existence. `Tool Items` carries no `Created At` on the ground that the log's first row holds the instant, so there is no second place holding it and no way to recover it. **Writing a late `Created` row is refused on purpose**: its `Event At` would state a time that is not when the tool item was created, and an append-only log whose rows record what was true at a moment cannot carry a row that does not. So the screen names those ids separately and the state stands — the form did until #449, and the page a registration lands on does since. The log pass also STOPS at its first failure rather than trying the rest, which is `createToolItems`' own posture and reason: a log write that fails is failing systemically far more often than per row.

**NOTHING BATCHES A CREATE, AND THIS IS THE ISSUE `createToolItems` NAMED AS OWNING THAT DECISION.** The figure it asked for, **measured on a dev server against the live base**: `registerToolItemsAction` costs **5 + 3N operations** for a new tool and 4 + 3N for one that already exists. Confirmed at three sizes — 1 tool item under a new tool was **8** (3 list, 3 create, 2 find), 2 under an existing one **10**, and 12 under a new one **41**. The three per tool item are its own create plus the parent find and the create `generateChildId` makes for its log row; the fixed term is the session find, the two lists the page's own picker needs, and the duplicate lookup.

Batching the tool item creates ten to a request, which is Airtable's limit, takes 3N to about 2.1N — **the log rows cannot be batched at all**, since `generateChildId` mints one id per parent under one lock, so the un-batchable term dominates and the order of magnitude does not move. Against that, a failed batch of ten spends ten ids at once and makes the account the screen owes coarser. **The measurable condition for revisiting it:** if the log row ever stops needing a per-parent mint — a `Tool Log ID` that is not `{Tool Item ID}-{seq}` would do it — the un-batchable term goes and batching the creates becomes the whole cost. **#470 found the condition stated too narrowly**: the id kept its shape, but the first row of a tool item created moments earlier has no siblings to read, so it needs no per-parent mint at all, and both writes go ten to a request — `A registration in batches (#470)`.

**SO ONE SUBMISSION IS CAPPED, AND THE NUMBER COMES FROM ONE INVOCATION'S BUDGET.** Measured at 12 tool items: **9.0 seconds, so 0.75 s each**, which puts `MAX_TOOL_ITEMS_PER_REGISTRATION` at 100 around 75 seconds against a Vercel function's 300-second ceiling — a fourfold margin, and the figure the cap is chosen from rather than a round number. It is a local measurement and the real rate depends on the round trip, which is what the margin is for. **The refusal is not a dead end** — it tells the reader to register up to the cap and repeat, and the find-or-create path is what makes the remainder land under the same tool, so the cap costs a second submission and nothing else. **Since #470 the budget no longer binds it**: a hundred is 24 operations, and what holds the cap at 100 is what says it — the dialog's words and `MAX_LABELS_PER_REQUEST`, which prints the largest registration in one press.

**THE IDS ARE REACHABLE FROM THE SUBMISSION'S ANSWER AND FROM NOWHERE ELSE IN THE APP YET, AND THAT IS ACCEPTED RATHER THAN OVERLOOKED.** The account names every minted `Tool Item ID` rather than counting them, because the id is what gets printed onto a sticker and a count cannot be acted on. It arrives as the action's return value, so **a reload loses it** — the rows are on the base and #339 is what lists them. It is written down because the alternative reading is that somebody forgot. **THE SENTENCE THAT SAID THIS WAS AFFORDABLE BECAUSE PRINTING LABELS IS A LATER PHASE IS GONE (#353):** that phase arrived, and the account being the only place the ids appear together is now the reason the link to the label sheet has to leave from it. Losing them on a reload costs a page of the tool's own screen rather than nothing. **#449 IS WHERE ALL OF THIS STOPPED BEING TRUE**: a registration lands on its tool's page with the ids selected, which is where they are listed together now and where a reload keeps them — `Where a registration lands` below.

**AND THE JOB IS THE ACTOR'S OWN, WITH NO OFFICE CLAUSE, WHICH IS A SECOND PREDICATE ON PURPOSE.** `lib/toolRegistration.js:assignedJobsFor` is not `lib/deliveryAccess.js:accessibleJobs`: that one admits President and Admin to every job because invoicing and reconciliation are office work. This track does not pass through the office, and `Tool Log."Job"` is the job the event HAPPENED on — so an Admin assigned to no job has no such job and is refused, on the same screen and in the same words as anybody else with no assignment. `offline/tool-registration.mjs` holds the disagreement between the two predicates as an assertion, so a later pass that aligns them fails rather than quietly widening a write path.

### Where a registration lands (#449)

A registration that writes a tool item redirects to its tool's page, on the page of the list holding the first tool item it wrote — the first page since #463 — with every one it wrote selected. `lib/toolRoutes.js:toolPath` is the address — the record id, the page, the selection and, on this one address, a fourth argument carrying the registration's account; `lib/toolListView.js:pageHolding` picked the page until #463; `lib/toolRegistration.js` holds the account's reading, the form's prefill and every word; the fork is `app/(tools)/tools/[toolRecordId]/RegistrationShortfall.js`. `offline/tool-registration.mjs` and `offline/tool-list-view.mjs` hold them.

**THE ARRIVAL IS THE ACCOUNT NOW, WHICH IS #321 REACHING THE LAST SCREEN IT EXEMPTED.** #338's form stated what it wrote in its own answer because nothing else held the ids together, and `docs/briefs/_shared.md` called it the fifth exception and the shape to copy, since a reload repeated nothing. #443 is what removed the premise: a tool's page carries a selection in its address, which is exactly a set of ids that survives a reload and is one press from their labels. So a registration lands on a record after all — its tool — and the selection is the whole of what it says about the act.

**BOTH HALVES OF THE ADDRESS WERE ALREADY IN THE ACTION'S HAND.** `upsertTool` returns the row it found or made, so the record id costs nothing; that row's `Tool Items` array is the one it had before the batch — empty for a tool just created — so its length is the position the first new tool item takes. No read was added. **The residual is `withKeyLock`'s:** a registration of the same tool in another invocation between the lookup and the batch moves this one's tool items along, and the landing can open a page early; the selection is by id, so the list's own `N not on this page` still reconciles it. Unobserved, and written in the action's header.

**THE PAGE IS WHERE WHAT IT WROTE BEGINS, AND `pageHolding` SITS BESIDE `pageOfToolItems` SO THE TWO DECIDE ONE EDGE.** Page 1 would have been simpler and is wrong for any tool with twenty-five tool items already under it: the landing would show none of the new ones and read `14 selected, 14 not on this page`. The last one's page splits a run that spans pages the wrong way round. The first one's page reads the way the list does, and a run past it is what #443's sentence already reconciles. **#463 turned the list newest first, which makes that page the first**: what a registration wrote is the newest its tool holds, so it begins the list whatever the tool held, and `pageHolding` went with the arithmetic — `The tools screens' look (#463)`.

**WHAT THE LANDING CANNOT SHOW RIDES BESIDE THE SELECTION, AND IT IS AN ACCOUNT RATHER THAN A CONFIRMATION.** Two facts: how many were asked for and not written, and which of the tool items written have no `Created` row. The page cannot read either off the base — the asked-for count exists nowhere, and the unlogged tool items are the tail of a run whose rows may be on another page — so they ride in the address, in `offline/url-parameters.mjs`' account group. `/invoices/[invoiceId]?paired=` is the precedent: an action landing on the record it made, carrying the one thing about how it came to be that the record does not hold. A cookie or a hand-off in client memory would have been a channel no check reads and the first mechanism of its kind, and would lose the offer on the reload a phone makes on its own.

**THEY LIVE ON THE LANDING ADDRESS AND NOWHERE ELSE, AND THE LIST'S OWN WRITES ARE WHAT MAKE THAT TRUE.** `toolPath` takes them as a fourth argument and only the action passes one; a press of a box or a step writes three, so the reader's first move leaves them in the history entry they came from. A reload before that repeats both, which is accurate: nothing repairs a missing registration, and the fork stands until it is answered. `Done registering` removes `unwritten` from the CURRENT address, edited in place, because the boxes rewrite the address without a render and an address rebuilt from the render would put back a selection the reader had changed. **Carrying them through every press and step was the other shape and was refused**: the notice has no control, so nothing would ever take it out of the addresses the reader moves through, and an account following the reader around is what #321 removed. **The known leftover:** after `Register the other N` succeeds, Back twice reaches the landing that offered it; the form it opens still needs a press. `paired` has the same property. **Since #456 it is Back once**: the dialog opens over the landing without moving the address, so the landing that offered it is one entry back, the fork on it and the dialog shut until it is pressed again. **#455 renamed the two and gave the notice a control of its own**: `Done registering` is `Not now` and takes `asked` out with `unwritten`, `Register the other N` is `Create the rest`, and the notice's `Got it` takes `unlogged` out the same way — so the notice no longer rides every address the reader writes either, and still leaves only by a press. `The design's words (#455)` below.

**THE FORK IS TWO CONTROLS AND THE NOTICE NONE, AND THAT IS THE DIFFERENCE A DESIGN MAY NOT LOSE.** `Register the other N` opens the form filled in; `Done registering` ends the question. The dismissal read `Leave it as it is` in the design proposal and changed before any code: it is not this app's voice, and it did not say what it left — the shortfall or the notice beside it. `Done registering` pairs with `Register the other N` on the verb, one going on and one stopping, which is what says the choice is about the shortfall. The notice names tool items and offers nothing, because nothing repairs them; its sentence says `entered` where #338's said `came into existence`, since what is lost is the moment somebody entered the tool and not the moment it was bought. **When both happen they stand apart and whole**: the fork's count never includes the notice's tool items, which were written, and `Done registering` answers only the fork. **#455 kept the difference and dropped half the heading**: the design gave the notice `Got it`, one control that answers nothing and repairs nothing, so what a design may not lose is that the fork asks and the notice states — not that the notice has no control. The words above are #449's; the design's are in `The design's words (#455)`. **#459 stopped them standing together**: each is a dialog, told one at a time with the notice first, and each answer still takes only its own keys — `The account in dialogs (#459)`.

**THE FORM OPENS FILLED IN FROM ITS OWN FIELD NAMES, AND SAYS NOTHING ABOUT WHY.** `/tools/new?toolName=…&quantity=N` uses the form's own two field names. The name and not the record id: the form takes a name, a tool's name is its identity, and a record id in an address is the defect `naming.md` records — a query value is encoded, so the reason a name cannot be a path segment does not reach it. The count goes through `readQuantity`, so the form never opens on a number its own submit refuses. In the inventory both are NAVIGATION: a reload opens the same form on the same suggestion and writes nothing, and `quantity` is that group's first member naming no record, admitted on its reload answer. No sentence reads the address back, which is what keeps a copied link a request rather than somebody else's account. **#456 is where none of this is about an address any more**: the form is a dialog, opened with the name and the count by the control that opens it, and no address carries either.

**A REGISTRATION THAT WROTE NOTHING STAYS ON THE FORM, AND #338's WRITE ORDER IS WHAT MADE IT A CHOICE AT ALL.** The `Tools` row is found or created before the first tool item, so when the batch writes none there is always a tool page to go to — for a new tool it says `Nothing is recorded under this tool.`, the sentence written for this very state. Going there was weighed and refused: the landing's content is what was written, and with nothing written there is nothing to select, print or reload. The form returns `None of the N asked for were written. Registering again writes them under the same tool.` — true whether the tool was found or made — in the slot every refusal uses. **It also fixed a defect nobody had met**: an empty batch rendered `Registered 0 tool items of … on …` with a label link naming nothing. A THROW before the batch — from `upsertTool`, or from the day-prefix query — still reaches no slot, as before, and the action's header says so. **Since #456 it is the dialog that stays**, saying the design's `Couldn't create the tools. Try again.` on the line above its actions — `Couldn't add the tools. Try again.` since #485.

**AND A REFUSAL KEEPS WHAT WAS TYPED, WHICH THE NEW REFUSAL IS WHAT MADE WORTH DOING.** React 19 resets a form once an action bound through `action` settles. Measured on this form, hydrated, with the handler taken out: a refusal kept the name, a controlled input, and put the count back where the page started it and the job back to its placeholder. That cost nothing while the only reachable refusal was a name of spaces; `None of the N … Registering again …` asks for the same submit again. So the form submits through `onSubmit`, which prevents the default and dispatches inside a transition — read in the installed react-dom, its form-action listener then hands `startHostTransition` a `null` action, whose branch is a no-op where the other calls `requestFormReset` first — and keeps `action` for a press before hydration. Both halves were seen: without `action`, an unhydrated press became a GET of the form's address carrying every field; with it, the same press reached the action and came back with its refusal. **The other forms keep the reset**: `app/prs/[prId]/EditAndContinueForm.js` records it, on a form whose submission carries state rather than fields. **#456 dropped `action`**: a dialog cannot be open before hydration, so the press it was kept for cannot happen, and the handler is the whole submit.

**THE FIGURES, ON A DEV SERVER AGAINST THE LIVE BASE.** `registerToolItemsAction` is **46 operations for fourteen tool items under an existing tool and 7 for one** — `4 + 3N`, #338's figure unchanged, since nothing was added to the action. The redirect is **one round trip**: the POST answers `303` carrying the destination's render, which Next 16.2.10's action handler fetches on the server (`createRedirectRenderResult`), and the browser requests only the page's client chunks after it. The landing is **4 operations** — with a selection of fourteen, of one, with the account in the address, and with a malformed one. A press of `Done registering` sends no request.

**WHAT A BROWSER SHOWED AND WHAT IT COULD NOT.** At 1440, fourteen of `DEMO Angle Grinder` landed on page 1 of 2 with thirteen checked and `14 selected, 1 not on this page`, and a reload kept it; the step to page 2 showed the fourteenth alone and `14 selected, 13 not on this page` — **the first page boundary this base has shown a browser**, which #442 and #443 could not. At 375, the form opened from `?toolName=DEMO%20Angle%20Grinder&quantity=1` with both filled in and the preview naming the tool; an edit moved the preview, a reload restored the suggestion and wrote nothing, and one more landed on page 2 with `1 selected` and no sideways scroll. **The shortfall and the unlogged state were not produced** — each needs a write to fail against the live base. What was seen instead is how the page RENDERS their parameters, from addresses typed by hand: the fork and the notice together at both widths, the notice naming a tool item on the other page, `Done registering` removing only `unwritten` and sending nothing, a reload bringing back the notice and not the fork, a press of a box leaving the notice behind in the address, `Register the other 3` opening the form filled in, and `unwritten` of 100, 0 or a word showing no fork. That the action writes those parameters on a real failure is held on the AST, not seen.

**THE BASE GAINED FIFTEEN TOOL ITEMS AND FIFTEEN `Registered` ROWS UNDER `DEMO Angle Grinder`, KEPT** — `Created` rows since #455 renamed the option in place: twenty-seven under it now, the first tool on this base past a page. Removing them would free today's highest ids for reuse — the hazard `The tool item ID` section records — and retiring them would write fifteen events that never happened. Two `Auth Tokens` rows were minted for the sign-ins and one went unused: `localhost` already carried a session, a second account there would have signed it out, and so both landings ran as the same account and the one-job form was not the one registered from. `127.0.0.1` could not stand in, because Next 16 refuses its dev resources to any origin but `localhost` unless `allowedDevOrigins` names it, so a page there renders and never hydrates.

**WHAT THE CHECKS HOLD.** By value, at their edges, each edge first shown to be one: the page a position lands on (24 and 25, 49 and 50, read off `pageOfToolItems` before `pageHolding` is asked — until #463 removed it), the account's reading (1 and 99 kept, 0 and 100 refused), the prefill's (100 kept, 101 at 1), both addresses and every new word. Off the AST, beside a planted file doing each wrong: the redirect's record, page, selection and account; the redirect outside every `try` and after the empty batch's refusal; every return a refusal; the form reading nothing off its state but `error`, starting from the prefill, and submitting through the handler with `action` kept; the page reading `page` and the account and never `id`, and what it hands the fork and the notice; and the fork's dismissal deleting one key from the current address. **Forty-two mutations were run and all forty-two fail the tier**; one would have survived — `registerPath` dropping a count of one — and was caught by reading the mutation list against the literals before it was run, so the edge it needed went in first. **#456 rewrote `offline/tool-registration.mjs` for the dialog**: the prefill's edges are `openingCount`'s, the address went with the route, and the dialog reads `fields` beside `error` and carries no `action` — `The registration as a dialog` has what it holds now.

### Registering more of a tool from its own page (#451)

A tool's page carries a control that opens `/tools/new` with the tool's name filled in and no count. Nothing new was needed to make the address or to read it: `registerPath` already wrote a name alone, and `readRegistrationPrefill` already started a count it was not given at 1. The page gained one link and `TOOL_REGISTRATION_COPY` one word, `registerMore`; `offline/tool-list-view.mjs` and `offline/tool-registration.mjs` hold both. **#456 made the control an opener of the dialog, on this tool and with no count, and took `registerPath` and `registerMore` with the route.**

**IT IS NOT THE SECOND PATH #338 REFUSED.** That issue kept one form because buying more of a tool is the same act as buying the first, and two forms would be two write paths for one rule. This opens the one form, whose write finds the tool by its name exactly as a typed name would and whose preview says so before anything is written. A control that registered from this page by itself — a count beside the list, say — is the shape that paragraph refuses.

**NO COUNT, AND THE FORM STARTS AT 1 FOR THE REASON IT STARTS THERE FOR A COUNT IT REFUSES.** Nothing on this page knows how many were bought, so the address carries none, and `readRegistrationPrefill` reads an absent count the way it reads `quantity=150` or a word: the form starts where it starts with nothing named. A blank count was weighed and refused. The plain form from `/tools`, a count the submit refuses and this control all arrive with no count the form accepts, and a blank for one of them would open one form two ways — the first of them being #338's form, which this issue has no reason to change. **#456 kept the start and took the address**: `openingCount` reads what an opener hands the dialog through `readQuantity`, as `readRegistrationPrefill` read the query, and with no address a refused count has no way in — the list's opener and this one hand none, and those two are the ways a blank would split.

**THE WORDS ARE `Register more of this tool`, AND WHAT DECIDED THEM IS THE CONTROL THAT CAN STAND BESIDE THEM.** On a landing that fell short, the fork's `Register the other 3` opens the same form on the same tool, so the two differ only in what they are about: the fork's finishes the registration the reader just made and this one begins another. `Register more` alone reads as the rest beside it; `of this tool` names the tool rather than a registration. The form's heading, which `/tools`' control carries, was the other candidate and fails twice — it opens the form on no tool, and its `tool items` is decided against appearing anywhere new (#378). The words are `TOOL_REGISTRATION_COPY`'s rather than `TOOL_LIST_COPY`'s because they are about the screen they open, which is the label screen's arrangement with its own two openers. **On a tool with nothing under it, `more` reads as additional**, which is all of them; a second voice for that state would be a second word for one act. **#455 took the design's verb and the argument held**: `Create more of this tool` beside the fork's `Create the rest`, which still differ in what they are about — one begins a registration, one finishes it. **#456 took the design's word for it, `New tools`, and neither of the heading's two failures stands**: the dialog it opens is on this tool, and `tool items` left the heading in #455. It still differs from `Create the rest` in the way this paragraph needs. **#485 made the pair `Add tools` and `Add 2 more`**, which still differ that way: the fork's names how many are left of the registration it finishes, and this one begins another.

**IT STANDS UNDER NO CONDITION, AND THE EMPTY TOOL IS WHERE THAT MATTERS.** The control sits above the branch that swaps the list for its empty state, so a tool a failed registration left standing alone keeps the one act that fills it, with the name that registration found or made already typed. Nothing else about the control changes there — the words and the address are the same on every tool — which is why the answer to whether it is especially useful there is where it sits rather than a variant of it. **Since #463 the empty state draws a second, bordered opener under its sentence**, 1b's, beside the head's; this said the swap was for `Nothing is recorded under this tool.`, the sentence that went with it, and was corrected per #181.

**AND IT IS DRAWN FOR A READER ON NO JOB, WHICH IS THE DECISION MOST WORTH KNOWING THE GROUNDS OF.** Hiding it would cost nothing — the page already holds the session's user and the whole job list, and `canRegisterToolItems` is the question — and was refused. The other two controls that open this form, `/tools`' own and the fork's on this very page, are drawn for every reader, as is `/deliveries`' `Record a delivery`, whose form has a refusal screen of its own for a reader it finds no job for; #338 made `/tools/new` for that reader the heading and `noJob`, the screen being the refusal and saying what to do about it. Gating this control alone would give "may this person register" two answers on one screen. **The one opener this app hides is `/invoices`' `New invoice`**, and the comment above it states the reason as a rule: an employee must not be offered a button that lands on a refusal. That refusal is a role — the office's work, refused to a site employee for as long as they are one — where this one is an assignment the refusal tells the reader how to get. The tool item page's controls are absent for a reader on no job as well, and they are the write itself rather than a way to a screen that answers that reader. **If the invoice rule is ever read as reaching assignments**, this control, `/tools`' and the fork's move together, and `canRegisterToolItems` is the one predicate all three would ask. **#456 is that reading, and it kept both halves of this paragraph**: the three openers moved together, each drawn disabled with `Ask the office to assign you to a job` before it, none hidden, all asking `canRegisterToolItems` — only where the reason is said changed, from a screen arrived at to the control that cannot act.

**WHAT CARRYING THE NAME COSTS WAS #449's, AND IT IS WRITTEN WHERE #449 DECIDED IT.** The address names the tool and not its row, so on a base holding two `Tools` rows with one key — `withKeyLock`'s residual (#338) — the write lands under whichever row the lookup returns. The fork had that before this issue, when the registration that fell short made the second row; this control is `registerPath`'s second caller and meets it on the other row's page. It is said once, in `registerPath`'s docstring, and this page's header points there. **#456 kept the cost and moved where it is said**: the name arrives as a hidden field rather than in an address, and `registerToolItemsAction`'s header says it once, which this page's header points to. Unobserved: the base's four tools carry four distinct keys.

**THE FIGURES, ON A DEV SERVER AGAINST THE LIVE BASE.** The tool's page is **4 operations** with the control — on both pages of `DEMO Angle Grinder`, with a selection, and with a registration's account in the address — which is #339's figure unchanged, since the name is on the row the page already read. The form it opens is **3**, as it is from `/tools`. Pressing a box and pressing `Done registering` sent no request.

**WHAT A BROWSER SHOWED AND WHAT IT COULD NOT.** At 1440 and at 375 the control on page 1 and page 2 of `DEMO Angle Grinder` opens `/tools/new?toolName=DEMO+Angle+Grinder`, and it still did after a box on page 2 was pressed and the step to page 1 carried the selection — `Print labels` carried it and the control did not. On a hand-typed landing with `unwritten=3` the control stood before the fork, whose link carried `&quantity=3`; `Done registering` took the fork and `unwritten` away and left the control. The form it opened held the name, 1, and `DEMO Angle Grinder is already a tool.`, and nothing was submitted. **An empty tool and a reader on no job were not seen**: every tool on the base has a tool item under it, and a session for a reader with no job is a base write.

**WHAT THE CHECKS HOLD.** By value: the word, and the prefill of a name alone, both directly and through `registerPath` and the browser's own parser. Off the AST: one control on the page, handed `tool.toolName` and nothing else, saying `TOOL_REGISTRATION_COPY.registerMore`, under no condition — beside a planted page carrying it in the not-found return, beside the list only and for a reader on a job only, each seen under its condition, and the one beside the list seen handing a record id and a count and saying the heading. **Ten mutations were run and all ten fail the tier.** Three of them — the control moved into the list's branch, gated on the reader having a job, and carrying a count of one — leave every screen this base can render as it was, and fail only on the source. **#456 rewrote these for the dialog's openers**, and `The registration as a dialog` has what they hold now.

### The registration as a dialog (#456)

The registration is a dialog over the page that opens it, and `/tools/new` is gone. `app/(tools)/tools/RegistrationDialog.js` is the dialog and its opener, drawn by `/tools`, by a tool's own page and by the offer a registration that fell short makes there; `registerToolItemsAction` sits beside it in `app/(tools)/tools/actions.js`. The frame is `app/components/DialogFrame.js`, the controls `app/components/Controls.js` and the list they open `app/components/Menu.js`, their words in `lib/dialogFrame.js` and `lib/controls.js`; `offline/dialog-frame.mjs`, `offline/tool-registration.mjs` and `offline/tool-list-view.mjs` hold them.

**EACH OPENER HANDS OVER WHAT THE ADDRESS CARRIED, AND NOTHING IS WRITTEN TO THE ADDRESS.** The list opens it on no tool, with the tools it already loaded for its rows; a tool's page opens it on that tool; the offer opens it on that tool with the count it did not write. Opened on a tool, the name is the line under the title rather than a field — the design's — and it submits as a hidden field, so what was a name the reader could change there (#449, #451) is the tool the page is about; another tool is typed from the list. `offline/url-parameters.mjs` lost the route's two parameters and gained none. **No redirect stands at the old address**: nothing printed carries it — a label carries `/t/` — and nothing was deployed, so a request for it reaches the dynamic segment, which takes `new` for a record id and finds none.

**IT IS OPEN WHILE THE ADDRESS IS THE ONE IT WAS OPENED AT.** The action ends in a redirect, and a redirect back to the page the dialog was opened from — a tool's page landing on itself with a new selection — renders that page again with the dialog's state kept, so a dialog that closed only when told would stand open over its own landing. Opening records the address, a landing moves it, and the dialog is gone. Nothing else moves the address while it is open: a modal dialog leaves the page behind it inert, the list's boxes and steps included.

**THE BROWSER'S MODAL DIALOG, FOR WHAT IT GIVES WITH NO CODE.** `showModal` makes the rest of the page inert to pointer and keyboard both, lifts the dialog into the top layer, turns Escape into a `cancel` event and hands focus back to the opener when it closes — every behavior CLAUDE.md asks of something opening over the page, with no focus trap written and no dependency. **#459 is the one case it does not cover**, a dialog no press opened, and the frame hands that one's focus to the page's heading. The frame adds what 0l asks beyond it: the caret in the first field that takes typing, no closing while a submission is in flight — Escape and the close both ask the owner, which the frame does not do while busy — and nothing on a press of the backdrop, which 0l does not draw closing and which would lose what was typed.

**THE LIST A FIELD OPENS IS 0a's MENU, IN THE TOP LAYER, AND NOTHING WAS ADDED TO MAKE IT.** A native select draws the platform's list, which is not the design's, so the job is WAI-ARIA's select-only combobox and the name its editable combobox with list autocomplete and manual selection, both over one `Menu`. The list is a `manual` popover placed under its field from the field's box, so a dialog body that scrolls cannot clip it, and it stays interactive because a modal dialog's inertness follows the DOM and the list is inside the dialog there. What each key does is `lib/controls.js`'s, a pure function per pattern and held by value, and the components only apply it. #458's two dialogs reach the same list: the job is a `Choice` in both, and the check-out's name this `Combobox`.

**WHAT A SUBMISSION MAY BE IS ONE FUNCTION, ASKED TWICE.** `readRegistration` refuses in the dialog before anything is sent and in the action again, since a Server Action is reachable without a screen. A refusal about one field is keyed by its name and said under it, all of them at once as 1j draws them; a refusal about the whole registration — a reader on no job, a batch that wrote nothing — is the one line above the actions. A field's refusal goes as soon as that field changes, and the one about the whole dialog stays until the next press. The dialog's own refusal of a press and the action's answer take one place, and what stands in it is the last press's.

**A READER ON NO JOB NEVER OPENS IT.** Every opener asks `canRegisterToolItems`, and for a reader it refuses each is drawn disabled with `Ask the office to assign you to a job` before it (0f). #451 kept every opener drawn and all three on one predicate, and both stand; what changed is where the reason is said — beside the control that cannot act, rather than on a screen a reader arrives at to be refused, which is also `/invoices`' rule. The action refuses such a reader in the same words, for a caller with no screen.

**THE COUNT BESIDE A TOOL IS EVERY ITEM UNDER IT.** A suggestion and the preview both say `N items`, and N is the length of the tool's `Tool Items` link — the figure its own page heads its list with — rather than In stock and Out, as the design counts it, because one word showing two figures on two screens reads as a discrepancy (`design-system.md`, answer 12). `/tools` already holds that array for its three counts, so the figure costs no read and no fourth map joins `lib/toolStatus.js`.

**TWO FRAMES STOOD ON THIS AXIS UNTIL #458, AND THE CONDITION WAS A CHECK.** The tool item page's retirement and check-out name sheet were still on `app/components/modalStyles.js`, and `offline/dialog-frame.mjs` named exactly those two, so a third drawn on the old frame failed. #458 moved them onto this frame and emptied the list: the check now fails any file under `app/(tools)/` that imports the old frame. The screens above this axis keep `modalStyles.js` until #258, and `app/admin/disciplines/new/JobCombobox.js` — a searchable combobox written for its one form — goes when they take these controls, which `Controls.js`' header records.

**A PAGE THAT BECAME A DIALOG KEEPS ITS BRIEF.** `tools-new.md` says where the dialog opens, in an `Opens from:` line in place of `Route:`, and `offline/screen-briefs.mjs` holds such a brief to the component that draws it and to the pages it names, both ways: every page it names draws the dialog, and every page that draws it is named.

**THE FIGURES, ON A DEV SERVER AGAINST THE LIVE BASE.** `/tools` is **4 operations**, one more than #339's 3: the job list is read beside the tools, for the opener's question and the dialog's choice. A tool's page stays at **4** with both of its openers, since it read the reader and the job list already. `registerToolItemsAction` is **7** for one tool item under an existing tool, from `/tools` and from the offer alike — `4 + 3N`, #338's figure unchanged. A refusal the dialog makes sends nothing at all, and `/tools/new` is the tool page's not-found at **2**.

**WHAT A BROWSER SHOWED AND WHAT IT COULD NOT.** As `soo@` at 1440 and 375, on the dev server already running in this checkout:

- **The frame, against 0l**: 420 wide and 24 all round, the title 16 on 24 at 600 with its line 2 under it at 13 in Ink 2, the close 28 square, centered on the title's line and 4 out, the quantity 120 with the job 12 beside it, the actions 8 apart on the right. It draws in Instrument Sans, loaded as one variable face, and the screen behind it in Arial.
- **The lists**: the job's and the name's each at the field's width, 6 under it and 5 inside, rows 36, under Raised, and drawn over the dialog's own actions — the top layer, unclipped.
- **The keys**:
  - The choice opened on Down with the chosen option under visual focus, stopped at its ends and chose on Enter. Escape shut the list and left the dialog open.
  - The typed name took a suggestion on Down and Enter without submitting.
  - A name typed in full said `Adds to DEMO Angle Grinder, which already has 27 items`, which is what that tool's page heads its list with, and a name no tool holds said `Creates a new tool`.
- **The refusals**: an empty name, 140 and no job were refused all at once, each under its field in red with the field ringed, and nothing was sent. A change to a field took only that field's refusal away. The count kept digits only, `1a40` reading `140`, and its step down from 140 gave 139.
- **Closing**: Escape, the close and Cancel each closed it and gave focus back to the opener, a press on the backdrop did nothing, Tab stayed inside, and an opening started from what its opener handed over rather than from what the last one was left holding.
- **Opened on a tool**: from a tool's page and from the offer, the tool's name was the line under the title and there was no field for it; the offer opened at its count, 2, and with one job that job was already chosen.
- **The phone**: at 375 the dialog was 319 wide with no sideways scroll; at 375 × 400 its body scrolled with a Rule at the edge its content was hidden past. **In a pane that draws no frames, neither the Rule nor a scroll moves until one is drawn** — a ResizeObserver and a scroll event are both delivered with a frame — so it was read after a screenshot drew one.
- **Two registrations**:
  - From `/tools`, choosing 26-DEMO-01 of two jobs, it landed on page 2 of `DEMO Angle Grinder` with `HYE-TL-260930-001` selected.
  - From a hand-typed offer, `?asked=5&unwritten=2`, with one job and the count stepped down to 1, it landed on the same page with `-002` selected and the dialog gone over its own landing. Back once reached the offer again.
- **A reader on no job**: `soo@`'s `Assigned Jobs` was set to one job, then to none, then back to its two through the Airtable API, and read back. With none, the list's opener, the tool page's and the offer's were each drawn disabled with `Ask the office to assign you to a job` 14 before them at 13 in Ink 3, named as their description, at both widths, and no dialog was rendered.

**Not seen**: a submission in flight, which ended faster than it could be read, and a batch that wrote nothing — that takes the base failing.

**THE BASE GAINED TWO TOOL ITEMS AND TWO `Created` ROWS UNDER `DEMO Angle Grinder`, KEPT**: `HYE-TL-260930-001` and `-002`, In Stock on 26-DEMO-01, for #449's reason — removing them would free today's highest ids. No `Tools` row was written, since both found the tool, and `soo@`'s assignments are what they were.

**WHAT THE CHECKS HOLD.**

- `offline/dialog-frame.mjs`: both patterns' keys by value; each control's wiring and the list's; the frame's modal, its Escape, its busy state and its caret; their words; and the two files still on the old frame.
- `offline/tool-registration.mjs`: each behavior of the dialog as the expression that decides it — what its fields start from, the line under its title, where a name is asked for, the words a count is said in, the gate at the opener and the address it is open at.
- `offline/tool-list-view.mjs`: the three openers' props, and the count the list hands the dialog.

**Fifty-one mutations were run, and thirty-eight failed the tier at first.** Twelve survived, and each was a check's: the one saying the dialog closes on a moved address read only that the address was read. One more did not match its string. Every check was rewritten to fail on its mutation, and all fifty-one fail the tier now.

### The account in dialogs (#459)

A registration's account — the fork for a shortfall and the notice for tool items with no first log row — is two dialogs over the tool's page, the design's 1k. `app/(tools)/tools/[toolRecordId]/RegistrationShortfall.js` and `RegistrationUnlogged.js` are the two, `lib/toolRegistration.js:accountToTell` says which is told, and the frame gained what they needed. `offline/tool-registration.mjs`, `offline/tool-list-view.mjs`, `offline/dialog-frame.mjs` and `offline/design-values.mjs` hold them.

**ONE AT A TIME, THE NOTICE FIRST, AND 1k DOES NOT DRAW THE TWO TOGETHER.** Its states are `Fewer written` and `Not recorded`, one or the other. The order is decided by where the fork's answer can go: `Create the rest` can end on another landing, whose address carries its own registration's account and no other, so a notice waiting behind the fork would be dropped unseen, and nothing else names those tool items together. The notice's one answer stays on the page, so it goes first. Each answer still deletes its own keys and no others (#455): after `Got it` the fork follows, and a reload between them opens whichever is left.

**THE ADDRESS DECIDES WHICH IS OPEN, AND A `done` FLAG WOULD HAVE HIDDEN A LATER LANDING.** A dialog is open while the page found its part and its key is still on the address — `accountToTell`, asked of the render's reading and of `useSearchParams()`, which a `replaceState` moves (#443). The `done` state #449 and #455 kept survives a landing on the same route: Next keys a page's state on its segment without the query (`createRouterCacheKey(…, true)` in 16.2.10's `layout-router.js`), which is what kept #456's dialog open over its own landing, so a fork answered once would have stayed hidden on the next registration's landing on that tool. Read in the source and not seen, since producing a shortfall takes the base failing; the component's header carries it.

**THE CLOSE AND ESCAPE ARE EACH DIALOG'S OWN ANSWER.** 1k sends its close to the handler `Not now` and `Got it` use, and the frame makes Escape the close, so no way out of either leaves its key on the address with nothing on the screen. A press on the backdrop still does nothing: the mock's closes, 0l draws no such thing, and #456 decided it for every dialog on the frame.

**`Create the rest` PUTS THE FORK AWAY AND OPENS THE REGISTRATION, AND CANCELING THAT BRINGS THE FORK BACK.** 1k's own handler closes the offer and opens the form, with no stacking. The question is unanswered until `Not now` and the address still carries it, so a canceled registration returns to the fork, which is what a reload would show; a registration that lands answers it by moving the address. The registration's rule that it is open only at the address it opened at is #456's and has one implementation, `useRegistrationOpening`, with the gate (`RegistrationOpener`) and the dialog (`RegistrationForm`) exported beside it: the fork's opener stands in a dialog that closes as the registration opens, so it cannot be the component that holds it.

**A DIALOG NOTHING OPENED HANDS FOCUS TO THE PAGE'S HEADING.** The browser returns focus to whatever held it when the dialog opened — nothing on a fresh load, an opener the landing took away after a registration from `/tools`, this page's own `New tools` after one from here. The heading is where the page begins, which is where an arrival with nothing to tell leaves the reader, and a screen reader names it on arrival. The frame makes it focusable and moves focus there for a dialog marked `unprompted`, one implementation whatever the page, and CLAUDE.md's overlay rule carries the clause. On opening, focus is the browser's — the close, the first focusable thing in a dialog with no field — and a dialog that opens as another closes takes focus as it opens.

**THE WORDS ARE 1k's AS FAR AS #321 ALLOWS.** 1k's fork says `2 couldn't be created. The 3 that were are saved and selected on this page.` and its notice `All 5 were created and work as usual. Only the creation date wasn't saved for these 2, and it can't be added later.` The two sentences that account for what was created are left out: the selection is the arrival's whole confirmation, and a line read off the address repeating it is what #321 took off every screen — and the notice's needs a count a landing with no shortfall does not carry. The rest went in: the fork's period, and the notice's `these 2` and `, and it can't be added later`, which is `WHAT AN UNLOGGED TOOL ITEM IS` above said to the reader. `offline/tool-registration.mjs` fails a string saying either of the two left out.

**THE NOTICE READ THREE NAMES FIRST AND DECLARED THREE, WHICH #462's TABLE SAID IT WOULD NOT.** 1k lists the ids in the id face on a dialog's summary — 0l Split's card, the Field ground at the Group radius, 16 inside above and below and 18 either side — with 4 between two ids, a figure no row states. `--color-background-muted`, `--font-id` and `--tracking-id` were marked for #457 and are read here first. `--spacing-dialog-summary-inset-y`, `-inset-x` and `-stack` are declared, named for the summary rather than for spacing in general so another screen's line spacing does not borrow them. Fragment Mono loads in the tools layout beside Instrument Sans, in its one weight. `design-system.md` carries the correction.

**THE FRAME GAINED FOUR THINGS.** `unprompted`. `DialogMessage`, 0l's one sentence at 14 in Ink, which names itself as its dialog's description — a dialog with no field would otherwise be announced by its title alone, and #458's confirmations say the same kind of sentence. `DialogSummary`. And actions that wrap, still on the right: a disabled action carries its reason before it (0f), and a reader on no job meets the fork's `Create the rest` that way, which with `Not now` beside it is wider than the line in a dialog 420 wide as well as 319. A figure in a title or a sentence is tabular, as the design sets figures. #458 used one of them, `DialogMessage` for the retirement's sentence: nothing on the tool item page opens unprompted or holds a summary, and its actions fit one line at a desk.

**#318's MODAL RULE WENT IN THIS ISSUE.** CLAUDE.md held that a modal is for an act that cannot be undone and an act that can is edited in place; #456's registration was already a dialog outside it, and where a dialog goes is the design's. The notes that recorded it as a decision say so where they stand — #318's own in `deliveries-and-invoices.md`, and #362's and #363's here — and the comments that cited it were corrected; the tool item page's shape stood until #458 put its transitions and its retirement in the design's dialogs.

**THE FIGURES, ON A DEV SERVER AGAINST THE LIVE BASE.** The tool's page is **4 operations** with either dialog or both, as before. An answer sends no request: across two Escapes, one per dialog, no resource was fetched.

**WHAT A BROWSER SHOWED AND WHAT IT COULD NOT.** As `soo@` at 1440 and 375, on the dev server already running in this checkout, on `DEMO Impact Driver` with every landing typed by hand and `HYE-TL-260909-002` and `-003`, ids the base holds, named as the ones with no log row — the account is not checked against the base:

- **Both**: the notice first, titled `1 tool has no creation date` with the tool under it; Escape took `unlogged` out and nothing else and opened the fork; a reload opened the fork; `Not now` left the selection alone in the address, no dialog, and focus on the heading. With two ids, `×` took both out and the fork followed.
- **The fork alone**: the fork, and again after a reload; Escape left the selection alone and focus on the heading.
- **The notice alone**: the notice, and again after a reload; `Got it` left the selection alone and focus on the heading.
- **Nothing**: no dialog, the selection and `2 selected`.
- **`Create the rest`**: the fork shut and the registration opened on the tool at 3, the caret in the count; its Cancel and Escape each brought the fork back with the address as it was. Nothing was submitted.
- **Against 1k**: 420 wide and 24 all round; the sentence 14 on 20 in Ink, wrapped pretty, 20 under the title's line; the summary 16 and 18 inside at a radius of 10 on Ink at 4.5%, 14 under the sentence; the ids in Fragment Mono, loaded, 14 on 20 at −0.28px and 4 apart; the fork's `Not now` 89 by 36 and bordered and `Create the rest` 130 by 36 and filled, 8 apart and 24 under the sentence.
- **The phone**: 319 wide with no sideways scroll, the notice's sentence on two lines and the fork's actions on one; Tab reached `Got it` and `Not now` and Enter answered each.
- **The backdrop**: a press on it left the fork open.
- **A reader on no job**: `soo@`'s `Assigned Jobs` was set to none and back to its two through the Airtable API, and read back. With none, the fork's `Create the rest` was disabled with `Ask the office to assign you to a job` before it and the actions wrapped — `Not now` on a line of its own, the reason and the button under it — at 1440 as at 375, with nothing past the dialog's edge; the page's own `New tools` was disabled too.
- **The page's own openers**: the tool page's and `/tools`' each opened the registration and gave focus back on Escape, as before.

**Not seen**: a shortfall or an unlogged tool item a registration really produced — each takes the base failing — and a landing on the same tool's page after an answer, which is where the `done` flag would have hidden the next account.

**THE BASE GAINED NOTHING.** No row was written. `soo@`'s `Assigned Jobs` reads `26-DEMO-01` and `26-DEMO-02` again, in that order.

**WHAT THE CHECKS HOLD.**

- `offline/tool-registration.mjs`: `accountToTell` by value — both parts, each after the other's answer, each alone, none, and a forged count — beside a planted case showing the order is a choice; the words; and that no string says the two sentences left out.
- `offline/tool-list-view.mjs`: what each dialog is handed, and that the notice stands first; each told by `accountToTell`, open and closed as its own answer, unprompted; the fork shut while the registration is open and opening it through the one rule behind the one gate, at the count it was handed; the notice opening nothing.
- `offline/dialog-frame.mjs`: the heading's focus, only for a dialog marked so and in the branch that closes it; the sentence's description; the wrap; and the three dialogs on the frame.
- `offline/design-values.mjs`: the three names read here and the three declared, and #459 out of the waiting list.

**Twenty-seven mutations were run and twenty-six fail the tier.** The one that survives takes the id face's class off the layout's element while its loader stays, which the design-values check's own header names as what it cannot see — whether a loader's class sits above the element reading the face — and a browser showed the face loaded; taking the loader away fails it.

### A registration in batches (#470)

A registration writes its tool items ten to a request and then their `Created` rows ten to a request, and reads nothing before the second. `lib/airtableBatch.js` writes a run of rows in Airtable's batches and decides what a failed request counts as; `lib/airtable/client.js:createRecords` hands it the base. `lib/ids.js:generateFirstChildIds` mints a new tool item's first row, and `lib/idSequence.js:nextChildId` is the one composition it and `generateChildId` take an id from. `offline/airtable-batch.mjs`, `offline/tool-registration.mjs` and `offline/id-sequence.mjs` hold them, the first two against `_fakeBase.mjs`.

**THE ROW #338 CALLED UN-BATCHABLE HAD NOTHING TO READ.** #338 measured `4 + 3N` and left the creates one at a time because the log term could not shrink: `generateChildId` finds the parent and reads its siblings under the parent's lock before every row. The condition it wrote for revisiting that — a `Tool Log ID` not shaped `{Tool Item ID}-{seq}` — looked at the id and not at the parent. A tool item this invocation created a moment ago has no history, so the read returns nothing and `nextChildId` over no siblings is the id `generateChildId` would have minted: `-001`. The id kept its shape and its one implementation, and the row needs no find, no read and no lock. **A registration is `4 + 2⌈N/10⌉` now**, one more for each request that fails, and a hundred is 24.

**A FAILED REQUEST IS READ BACK BEFORE IT IS COUNTED, AND THAT IS WHAT KEEPS THE ACCOUNT RIGHT AT TEN TIMES THE GRAIN.** One row per request left a lost answer uncertain by one tool item; a batch leaves it uncertain by ten, and the landing's `3 of 5 tools created` and its unlogged notice are both read off that count. A refused request writes nothing — measured on this base for a batch destroy (#191) and a single update (#444), never for a create — while an answer lost after the commit reaches the caller as the same kind of error with every row there. So the batch writer does not classify a failure: it asks the base which of the failed request's minted ids it holds, counts exactly those, and sends nothing after. airtable@0.12.2 retries a 429 and nothing else, and Airtable did not process a 429, so no row is sent twice. The read rests on what the mint already rests on — `mintDailyIds` finds the day's highest id with a formula over the same field — and is spent only on a path that has already failed. `lib/airtableBatch.js`' header carries what is left: a read-back that fails too counts its batch as not written, since no screen has a word for not knowing and throwing would lose the account of every batch before it; a write landing after its own read-back is the window a single create always had; and a 200 whose body will not parse never settles at all.

**THE DAY'S IDS AND THEIR LOCK ARE AS #335 LEFT THEM, AND THE BATCHES ARE WHY THAT STILL HOLDS.** `mintDailyIds` is untouched — one lock on the day's prefix, one query, N contiguous ids, the create inside the lock. What runs inside it changed, and the property it has to keep is that the callback returns only once nothing it sent is in flight: the requests one at a time, a failed one's read-back included. A second registration in this process then reads the day's ids after every row of the first has landed or been counted out, and cannot mint them twice. Sending the batches together is the defect the shape refuses: `Promise.all` rejects on the first failure with the rest still on the wire, the lock opens, and the next mint reads the day before they land. Between invocations it is `withKeyLock`'s standing window, shorter than it was by the requests that are gone.

**THE FIRST ROW TAKES NO LOCK, AND ITS ONE RESIDUAL IS WRITTEN WHERE IT WOULD BE MET.** The lock makes a sibling read and the next create one critical section, and there is no read. Nothing else can be writing to the tool item either, since its id is shown nowhere until the landing. What is left is somebody guessing an id just minted — they are sequential — and recording an event on its page between the two passes, for which `generateChildId` mints `-001` too; unobserved, and in `generateFirstChildIds`' header. That function refuses a parent whose link array, as its create answered, is not empty, so the condition the shape rests on is checked rather than assumed.

**THE TWO PASSES STAYED IN SEQUENCE, BY CHOICE NOW.** #338 put the log pass after the batch because writing it inside would nest two locks. The pass takes none, so nothing forbids it; it stays because inside it would hold the day's lock — which every registration in this process waits on — through writes that mint nothing under it. A failure in it can still leave several tool items without a `Created` row, named on the landing as before.

**ONE INSTANT FOR A REGISTRATION'S `Created` ROWS.** Each carries the moment the log pass begins. Until #470 each carried its own write's moment, every one of them after the last tool item had been created, so none was nearer its event than this.

**THE CEILING STAYED AT 100 AND ITS DERIVATION DID NOT.** It came from one invocation's budget at three requests per tool item, and a hundred is 24 requests now. What holds it is what says it — the dialog's `Up to 100` and `Max 100 at a time.`, and `MAX_LABELS_PER_REQUEST`, which prints the largest registration in one press — so moving it changes both, and this issue changed no screen.

**THE FIGURES, ON A DEV SERVER AGAINST THE LIVE BASE**, against #338's formula and its rate — 41 operations in 9.0 seconds, about 0.22 s each — since the code before this was not run again:

| Tool items | Operations | `4 + 3N` | Action | At #338's rate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 6 | 7 | 1.73 s | about 1.5 s |
| 10 | 6 | 34 | 1.62 s | about 7.5 s |
| 11 | 8 | 37 | 2.18 s | about 8.1 s |

The action's time is Next's own `registerToolItemsAction … in` line. Each landing was its usual 4 operations, in 0.50 to 0.58 seconds of application code, and the whole POST, landing included, took 2.2 to 2.8 seconds. One tool item costs about what it did: one request fewer, and a create of ten is a slower request than a create of one.

**WHAT A BROWSER SHOWED.** As `soo@`, on a dev server in a worktree of its own on `:3200`, three registrations of `DEMO Rotary Hammer` on 26-DEMO-01 from the tool's own page — 1, 10 and 11 — each landing on that page with exactly what it wrote selected and no dialog: `HYE-TL-261001-001`; `-002` to `-011` and `10 selected`; `-012` to `-022`, `11 selected` and `23 items`. Read back afterwards in 2 operations, reading only: 22 tool items, each with exactly one `Tool Log` row, `{Tool Item ID}-001`, `Created`, on 26-DEMO-01, by one recorder, with no `Checked Out To`, and one `Event At` per registration — 1, 10 and 11 rows on three instants. **No failure was produced**: the base was not made to fail, and every failure above runs offline.

**THE BASE GAINED TWENTY-TWO TOOL ITEMS AND TWENTY-TWO `Created` ROWS UNDER `DEMO Rotary Hammer`, KEPT**: `HYE-TL-261001-001` to `-022`, In Stock on 26-DEMO-01, for #449's reason — removing them would free the day's highest ids. No `Tools` row was written.

**WHAT THE CHECKS HOLD.**

- `offline/airtable-batch.mjs`: how a run is cut, one request in flight, nothing in flight on return, a request refused whole, an answer lost with all or some of its rows landed, a read-back that fails, an answer in another order, and `createRecords`' wiring off its source — beside two wrong writers run against the same fake, and a planted wiring.
- `offline/tool-registration.mjs`: section 9 runs a registration's two passes against the fake and reads the landing they leave — a tool items request refused, its answer lost, a `Created` request refused, its answer lost, and both — and section 8 holds that the action and the two writers are wired as section 9 assumes.
- `offline/id-sequence.mjs`: `nextChildId` by value; `generateChildId` and `generateFirstChildIds` both taking their ids from it; the second with no lock and no read, refusing a parent with children; and its call sites in the enumeration.
- `verify-tool-item-ids-335.mjs`' Parts A and C moved to the batched shape, and were not run.

**Sixty-nine mutations were run and all sixty-nine fail the tier, and each of the 106 assertions this issue added or changed fails under at least one of them** — among the mutations, every extractor and the fake answering wrongly, so each planted copy's assertion is seen to fail too. The first run was sixty and left eighteen assertions no mutation failed. Nine were the round trip, whose label printed the ids it computed, so the mutation that failed one renamed it in the same breath; the label is fixed text now. Nine more mutations reached the other nine.

## The tool item's own page, and the address it spends (#340)

`/tool-items/[toolItemId]` renders the tool, the printed id, the status, the job and the whole of `Tool Log`. It is the first screen on this base reached by a machine rather than from a list. **It stood at `/tools/[toolItemId]` when #340 shipped** and that is what the next two paragraphs were written about; #348 moved it and gave the printed address a route of its own.

**THE BUDGET IS REAL AND IT IS NO LONGER THIS PAGE'S.** A QR carries the whole address, host included, and the symbol steps up a version as that string grows — printing finer modules on a sticker of the same size, which reads worse through oil and wear. None of the steps within reach is unreadable, so it is a budget rather than a limit; what makes it spent-once is that moving it means reprinting every label already glued to a tool. **Since #453 a step is a limit as well** — the label absorbs none, so a larger symbol is a label the labels' dialog refuses — and `The label for today's symbol` has why. #348 handed that budget to `/t/[toolItemId]`, which is what freed this page to be named for what it shows.

**AND NO LABEL IS PRINTED BEFORE THE APP HAS THE HOST IT WILL KEEP.** The host is part of the string the symbol encodes, so a move from a preview domain to a permanent one is the same reprint as a moved path. **The host was settled as of #348 — `hyeusa.com` — and is `app.hyeusa.com` now**, which #467 measured the address at; the app will run on a Vercel preview domain for a while yet, which is the state this paragraph is about: a label printed from a preview host encodes that host. It binds Phase 2 rather than this one. **#453 gave the rule a second reason**: the host's length now decides whether a label prints at all, and so which stock to buy — **a 12 mm tape since #467, so the first half is what is left**.

**THE LOOKUP IS CASE-INSENSITIVE AND THE PAGE REDIRECTS TO THE CANONICAL ID.** The label prints the tool item's code in readable characters under the QR code — the id less its token since #411, under the symbol since #431 — and that is not decoration: it is the fallback for a symbol somebody has scratched or painted over. A bare `=` — which #338 measured to be case-sensitive on a text field — made `hye-tl-260909-001` a miss, so the fallback amounted to demanding exact capitals from a gloved hand on a site. `getToolItemByToolItemId` now compares through `LOWER(TRIM(…))`, which is `getMaterialByKey`'s and `getToolByName`'s comparison rather than a third one.

**THE REDIRECT IS WHAT KEEPS THE CONCESSION FROM COSTING THE ADDRESS ITS UNIQUENESS**, and when #340 wrote this it was **the only page in the app that redirected at all**: every other `redirect()` under `app/` was in a Server Action or a Route Handler. `/t/[labelCode]` is the second, for a different reason and with a different status. The mechanism does reach a page render — `requireUser()` sends a reader with no session to `/login`, on every page — so what is new is a page deciding its OWN address needs to change. It is `permanentRedirect`, because the mapping is stable for good: a `Tool Item ID` is minted once and printed, the app offers no deletion, and `Retired` is what takes a tool out of the count while keeping its row, so nothing reassigns the string a cached redirect names. It runs after `requireUser()`, so a reader with no session learns nothing about which strings resolve.

**FIVE OPERATIONS PLUS ONE PER 50 LOG ROWS.** The session find, the tool item lookup, its tool, its people and the job list are each one, and only the log walk grows — in steps of 50 through `findChildRecords`, not per row, and skipping the parent find because the tool item record already carries its `Tool Log` array (#193). People and jobs are read once for the whole history rather than per row, so a tool item with two hundred events costs four operations there and nothing more on either. That shape is the point rather than the figure: this becomes the most frequently rendered screen on the axis the moment a scan opens it. **The one read that could be removed is the tool's**, by putting a `Tool Name` lookup on `Tool Items` — refused, because this base keeps removing that third copy rather than adding one, and the condition for revisiting it is #339's list needing the same read per row.

**THE PAGE READS THE CACHED `Status` AND `Job` AND COMPARES NOTHING.** It has the last log row in hand, so deriving them would be free — and it does not, for two reasons. They are what every other tools surface shows, so deriving here would let two screens say two things about one tool item; and they are present when the history is not, which is a state #338 can leave behind. **A comparison was designed and then removed before it was built.** A disagreement is reachable only by editing Airtable by hand, which this base forbids, since the only writer of the caches is the code that writes the row — and a sentence for it would need a reader who knows what a cache is, a design for where it sits, a brief entry and a check, all for a state no path produces. Code for an unreachable state outlives everyone's memory of why it is there.

**ALL FOUR OF A ROW'S FACTS ARE ON EVERY ROW**, which `lib/toolItemView.js:logRowFacts` states in code rather than only in the brief. `Event` is a closed select written with no `typecast`, `Event At` is stamped by the writer, `Job` is never blank and `Recorded By` is written on every path that appends — so a design hiding one, or folding a repeated `Job` away, is undoing a decision rather than styling one. **THIS SAID FOUR OF FIVE, WITH `Notes` AS THE ONE THAT DROPS, UNTIL #363 DELETED THAT FIELD** — see the retirement section for why the rule it was carried for went with it.

**A TOOL ITEM WITH NO HISTORY IS A REAL STATE AND THE PAGE SAYS SO.** #338's registration writes the tool item and then its first `Tool Log` row, reports a failure between the two, and repairs nothing — so a row can exist whose history is empty. `Tool Items` carries no `Created At`, so what is missing is the moment it came into existence, and there is nowhere else holding it. The sentence says that rather than that something failed: the row itself is sound, with its printed id, its status and its job.

**OLDEST FIRST, AND NOTHING SORTS.** `getToolLogByToolItem` returns the link array's order — creation order, which for an append-only table is chronological — and `/prs/[prId]`'s history, the only other one in the app, sorts ascending too. So the first entry is always the registration and the history begins where the tool item does. **A repeated `Job` down the list is the normal reading** rather than something to collapse: a tool item that never left its job carries the same job on every row, and that absence of blanks is what makes the previous row's job the previous job.

**`getToolItemByRecordId` WAS DELETED IN THIS COMMIT, AND `getToolItemsByRecordIds` WAS KEPT.** Both were written in #334 with no caller, which CLAUDE.md names as the shape `upsertMaterial` sat in from Phase 0 to #18 carrying three defects. The first has no foreseeable caller: this page and a scan both arrive with the PRINTED id, #339 reads a tool's items in a batch, and #341 is closed. The second is #339's — the count per status comes from flattening the `Tool Items` link arrays `getAllTools` already returns, which is one batched read rather than one call per tool, and that is what makes the no-rollup decision above affordable. Its docstring says so, so a later sweep for uncalled readers leaves it.

### The symbol on this page, and what it is for (#352)

The page draws the tool item's QR symbol and links to the label screen to print its label. Nothing new was written for either: the symbol comes from `buildToolItemQR` (#351) and the size from `labelBudget` and `symbolBox` (#353). **Since #457 it opens the labels' dialog on its own label**, which it builds once for the symbol and the dialog with `buildToolItemLabel`.
