The retirement is the third — every `Tool Log` row carries a job from the actor — so it and the picker's two words are `lib/toolJob.js` now, with `offline/tool-job.mjs` holding them and the deliberate disagreement with `canAccessJobDeliveries`. `canRegisterToolItems` stayed behind, because its name and its screen really are registration's. **That is `countsAsOrdered`'s path exactly**, which sat in `lib/materialPriceView.js` with the same condition written down until #169 fired it — the mechanism working twice rather than a coincidence.

**THE WRITE IS ONE FUNCTION FOR BOTH ACTIONS.** `writeEvent` holds the ordering, the try boundary and the report of a cache that did not move; the two actions differ only in which event they arrive with and how they got there. #362 had it inline and #363 would have copied thirty lines, which is the duplication CLAUDE.md's own section refuses to leave as two. It is not exported, so it is not a Server Action and not an entry point.

**THE FIGURES, MEASURED THE SAME WAY.** The page is **6 operations and unchanged again** — the session and the job list are already read, so the second offer costs nothing. `retireToolItemAction` is **7**, the same seven the scan action spends and for the same reasons: the session, the tool item, the job list, three for the log row and one for the cache.

**AND THE TWO LIST SCREENS HAVE NOW SEEN THE LAST STATE THEY HAD NEVER SEEN.** #339 recorded that `Out` and `Retired` had never been rendered with a figure in them; #362 showed the first and this shows the second. The `Notes` pair, which `logRowFacts` carried since #340 and which no row ever filled, is the one thing on this axis that was never rendered and now never will be.

**THE BASE CARRIES NO `Out` TOOL ITEM ANY MORE, AND THAT WAS SPENT DELIBERATELY.** `HYE-TL-260909-016` was the only one, and it was the only tool item whose job differed from the fixture account's — so retiring it as `scoped-fixture@`, who is assigned to `26-DEMO-01` alone, is the one run that could show the row taking `26-DEMO-02` from the tool item rather than `26-DEMO-01` from the actor. It did. Proving the write that this issue changed was worth more than keeping a count #362 had already rendered and recorded, and the alternative — checking another tool item out to keep one — would have recorded a scan that never happened.

## A refusal refreshes as it refuses (#378)

The two actions on the tool item's page re-render the page as they return a refusal, so the sentence and a page that agrees with it arrive together. `refuse` in `app/(tools)/tool-items/[toolItemId]/actions.js` is the whole of it; `lib/toolTransition.js`'s stale-page sentence changed with it, and `offline/tool-transition.mjs` holds both.

**THE DEFECT WAS THE FRAMEWORK'S DEFAULT AND NOT A MISTAKE IN THE BRANCH THAT SHIPPED IT.** A Server Action that only returns re-renders nothing: measured against the installed 16.2.10 rather than read off the documentation, `server/app-render/action-handler.js` sets `skipPageRendering` unless `workStore.pathWasRevalidated` says otherwise, so the flight response carries the return value alone. #362 returned `{ error }` and stopped, which left the refused reader looking at the status, the control direction and the history the page had rendered before the press — every one of them false — with one true sentence on top telling them to open the page again. **The app was asking a person to do the thing it could do itself.**

**EVERY REFUSAL GOES THROUGH ONE PLACE, AND THAT IS A CONCLUSION RATHER THAN A TIDINESS.** Both actions reach their verdict through `planTransition` on a status just read from the base, and the page reaches its own from the same function — so an action that refuses is one whose caller is looking at something the base no longer says. There is no refusal here that a re-render would not improve, so there is nothing to decide per branch. **`statusNotUpdated` is the case that needed it most**: it is the one answer that means something WAS written, and it used to stand on a page where the row it calls safe was nowhere to be seen.

**`refresh()`, NOT `revalidatePath` AND NOT `router.refresh()`.** The first does nothing here — a dynamic route passed to it with no `type` warns and has no effect, and this axis has no cached data to purge in any case. The second is a second round trip and puts the rule in the two forms rather than at the one place that decides to refuse. `refresh()` is a Server-Action-only call that sets that one flag, so the page is re-rendered into the same response the sentence rides in and `useActionState` keeps the sentence while the tree under it moves. **It is this app's first use of any of the three**, which is why the comparison is in the helper's header rather than only here.

**A SUCCESS NAVIGATES AND A REFUSAL RE-RENDERS IN PLACE, WHICH IS TWO ACTS RATHER THAN AN INCONSISTENCY.** A redirect throws away the client state it leaves — the scroll, an open modal, the chosen job, the slot the sentence lives in — and that is right when there is nothing to keep. After a refusal there is exactly one thing to keep. So #362's redirect stays exactly as it was, and the check holds the split from both sides: the module refreshes in one place, and that place is the helper.

**THE SENTENCE CHANGED BECAUSE ITS PREMISE DID.** It said the page was out of date and to open it again; the app opens it now, so the instruction had nothing left to do. What a freshly rendered page still cannot say is the two facts a reader needs, and they were the whole sentence: `Nothing was recorded. Somebody else scanned this first.` **A refused press and a successful one leave the same screen** — a flipped control, a moved status, one more history entry — and the only difference is whose entry it is, which is at the foot of the page — at its head since #463, whose design names that person and the entry's moment in the sentence itself (`The tools screens' look`). **It names no status**, which is this issue applied to its own sentence: the header says so directly above, freshly rendered, and restating it is the app declining to believe what it just did. **`scanned` rather than `moved`**, because `movesJob` already owns that word for a tool changing site and a reader meets both on one screen; and a scan is what this refusal is always about, since a retirement leaves a status that allows nothing and produces the terminal sentence instead.

**THE TERMINAL CASE PUTS NO SECOND SENTENCE BESIDE THE FIRST, AND THE REASON IS STRUCTURAL.** Measured in two tabs: with the dialog open on an `Out` tool item, retiring it from the other tab and then confirming the dialog refuses — and the fresh render puts the page into its `transition.refusal` branch, which removes both controls and the dialog with them. So the action's sentence has no slot to appear in, and what stands is the page's own `This tool item is Retired, so nothing more can be recorded against it.` They are the same string reaching the screen by two paths and only one path can be on screen at once. **Since #463 a desk shows nothing there** (1c) and a phone's foot bar says the design's `Nothing more can be recorded here.`, so the action's sentence and the page's are one string still. **The reader is told nothing about their own press, and that is right here where it would not be for a scan**: no control remains to be pressed in the wrong direction, and the fact they need is that the status is the end.

**THE FIGURES, MEASURED ON A DEV SERVER AGAINST THE LIVE BASE.** A refusal is **3 operations for the action and 6 for the render it triggers — 9 in one request**, where it was 3 and a false page before. The action's own share is unchanged, and both actions refuse at the same figure. The two scopes are sequential rather than nested, so `AIRTABLE_OPS_LOG` prints `recordToolItemEventAction — 3` and `/tool-items/[toolItemId] — 6` as separate lines. A success is unchanged at 7 for the action plus the 6 its redirect's GET spends. **The page a scan lands on is where a read costs most on this axis**, so the figure is stated rather than waved at: what it buys is that the refusal is the only one of the two answers that was ever wrong on screen.

**WHAT THE BROWSER RUN SHOWED AND WHAT IT DID NOT.** It showed the sentence arriving, the control flipping from `Check out` to `Check in`, the other tab's entry appearing in the history and the `useActionState` value surviving the re-render — all in one response, at 375, with the address unchanged. The refused presses wrote nothing: `HYE-TL-260909-018` carries three `Tool Log` rows, its first one and the two the other tab wrote. **It did not show `statusNotUpdated`, `jobNotYours` or the not-found refusal** — the first needs a cache write forced to fail and the other two need a forged submission, and both were ruled out rather than reached by editing source. That those three refuse through the same helper is held on the AST and is not a rendering claim.

**THE BASE SPENT ONE MORE TOOL ITEM, DELIBERATELY AND BY #363's PRECEDENT.** `HYE-TL-260909-018` went `In Stock` → `Out` → `Retired`, which is 13 `In Stock` and 5 `Retired` now. Reverting would mean writing a check-in describing an event that did not happen, which is what this file already refuses for a late `Created` row.

**`tool item` WAS STILL ON SCREEN AND THE NEW SENTENCE AVOIDED IT.** The screen words on this axis were `tool` and `tool item`, and the second was decided against appearing. This issue wrote one sentence and was not the pass that sweeps a word, which needed the replacement word decided first. **This heading said FOUR PLACES — `noTransition`, `retireOpener`, `retireHeading` and `movesJob` — and that was this screen's one constant counted as the axis**: nineteen strings said `tool item`, across five constants and a tab title, seven of them on this page alone. #455 was the sweep, with the design's word decided — see `The design's words (#455)`; corrected per #181 there.

## Who the tool went to (#376)

A check-out records the person who received the tool, and nothing else does. `Tool Log."Checked Out To"` is the field, `lib/toolTransition.js` holds the rule and the words, `getRecentCheckOuts` is the one read the page added, and `logRowFacts` grew a fifth fact that belongs to one event.

**TEXT RATHER THAN A LINK, BECAUSE THESE PEOPLE HAVE NO ACCOUNT.** Everyone in `Users` got there by signing in, and the people who take tools out on a site are not signers — #337 is why everyone who SCANS needs an account, and this is the other side of that boundary. So the value is what somebody typed, and `Recorded By` beside it is the one who did the typing.

**`Checked Out To` RATHER THAN `Received By`, AND THE NEAR-MISS IS THE REASON.** The parallel field on this table is `Recorded By`, so a participle-plus-preposition name was the obvious shape — and `Received By` differs from it by two letters, in a schema listing and in code (`receivedBy` / `recordedBy`) where the two sit on the same row of the same write. The chosen name says which event it belongs to instead, which is the fact a reader of the schema actually needs, and it is the same words the screen and the history entry say.

**IT IS NOT `Notes` COMING BACK, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS WHAT MAKES IT CHECKABLE.** #363 deleted `Tool Log."Notes"` because the rule it was carried for was dropped and no row had ever filled it — optional on every event, so nothing could be asserted about when it should be there. This is a FUNCTION OF THE EVENT: present on `Checked out` and absent on the other three, refused in both directions by `createToolLogEntry`. So `offline/tool-item-view.mjs` asks over the whole vocabulary, and a fifth event cannot arrive without somebody deciding which side it is on. An always-empty field and a field whose presence is a rule are not the same thing to keep.

**THE REQUIREMENT IS IN THE FORM AND IN THE ACTION AND AT THE WRITER, WHICH IS THREE PLACES FOR ONE RULE AND NONE OF THEM REDUNDANT.** A control constrains the person in front of it and a Server Action is reachable without one — `readQuantity`'s argument (#338), which #363 cited for the same reason. The writer is the third because that is where a never-blank invariant is actually kept, and #363 is what taught it: `createToolLogEntry`'s job guard was unreachable until one caller stopped resolving the value itself. **The form's half does not use `required`**, because the browser's own bubble is neither this app's sentence nor Design's to style; the submit is canceled in the form's own guard and `Checking out needs a name.` goes in the one slot every refusal on this screen arrives in.

**THE LIST IS LOADED ONCE AND NARROWED IN THE BROWSER, WHICH IS #338's ARRANGEMENT ONE SCREEN OVER.** The page loads the recent check-outs for every job its reader is assigned to; the picker decides which of them the list is about and typing narrows it further, both without a round trip. A query per keystroke is what that shape exists to avoid, and a query per picker move would have been the same defect in slower clothing.

**ONE OPERATION, AND ONLY WHERE A NAME IS ASKED FOR.** `getRecentCheckOuts` filters on the link's own text — see `airtable-access.md` for the measurement and for why the equality is right only on a single-record link — and caps at one page, so it is one operation whatever a job's history grows to. **Measured on a dev server against the live base: a check-out page is 7 operations and a check-in page is 6**, which is #340's recorded figure unchanged on every path that asks nobody — a check-in, a retired tool item, a reader with no job. The action is unchanged at 7.

**WHAT THE CAP COSTS, STATED RATHER THAN HIDDEN.** A person whose last check-out on this job is older than the hundredth most recent drops off the list. They can still be typed, and typing is what the list is a shortcut for.

**ONE ENTRY PER PERSON, ON THE KEY A TOOL'S NAME ALREADY USES.** `Mike R` and `mike r` are one person, folded on `itemNaming.js:textMatchKey` — normalize, then lower-case. **That composition moved into `itemNaming.js` in this issue** rather than being written a second time: `toolNameKey` was it spelled out, and a recipient's name is the second typed string on this axis folded the same way, so the two halves are one function with two named consumers. `toolNameKey` kept its name because it is about a TOOL's name, which is `canRegisterToolItems` staying behind when `assignedJobsFor` went. **The spelling shown is the most recent one**, which falls out of walking newest first rather than being a rule of its own — somebody who corrects how their name is written sees the correction.

**MEASURED IN A BROWSER, AT 375, WITH FOUR CHECK-OUTS ON THIS BASE.** Three tool items went out to `Dana K`, `Mike R` and `mike  r`; the fourth's sheet offered **two** entries, `Dana K` and `mike r`, most recent first and the double space collapsed — so the fold, the ordering and the normalization are one screenshot rather than three claims. Typing `mike  r` narrowed a two-entry list to one, which is the search ignoring case and spacing exactly where the de-duplication does. The base carries **9 `In Stock`, 4 `Out`, 5 `Retired`** now, and 4 `Tool Log` rows with a name and none on any other event.

**AND THE FIVE CHECK-OUTS WRITTEN BEFORE THIS ISSUE CARRY NO NAME, WHICH IS VISIBLE AND IS NOT REPAIRED.** `HYE-TL-260909-018-002` renders `Checked out to` with nothing under it. That is the same treatment the other four facts get — a blank is a defect upstream and the pair renders empty rather than disappearing and taking the defect with it — and writing a name onto those rows now would state something nobody recorded. The invariant holds forward.

**THE SHEET OPENS ON A PRESS AND NOT ON FOCUS, WHICH A BROWSER DECIDED.** Closing hands focus back to the field, so opening on focus reopened it the moment it closed and `Done` did nothing at all. A tap is a click, so the design's "touch the field" is unchanged; the keyboard gets `Enter` and `ArrowDown`, because CLAUDE.md requires anything that opens over the page to open from the keyboard and a text input does not give that for free.

**THE RECENT LIST IS ABSENT UNTIL A JOB IS CHOSEN, NOT EMPTY.** Also a browser finding: a reader with two assignments can open the sheet before touching the picker, and the empty sentence then said nothing had gone out on a job nobody had named. A person on one job never meets it, because that one is chosen from the start.

**`tool item` WAS STILL ON SCREEN**, which #378 recorded as four places and this issue did not change, and the words added here avoided it. The four were one constant's; the axis had nineteen, and #455 swept them once the design chose the word.

## The transitions and the retirement as dialogs (#458)

The tool item page's check-out, its check-in for a person on several jobs and its retirement open in the design's frame, and `ToolTransitionForm.js` and `RetireToolItemForm.js` are gone. In `app/(tools)/tool-items/[toolItemId]/`, `TransitionDialog.js` is the transition's press and its dialog, `RetirementDialog.js` the retirement's opener — `MoreActions.js` since #463, which put it in a menu — and `RetirementConfirm.js` its question, and `JobSheet.js` and `NameSheet.js` are 1f's two sheets. The frame gained the phone's sheet and 0l's Confirm, the controls their sizes in a sheet and the field a phone opens a sheet from, and the field's list a head. `offline/tool-transition.mjs`, `offline/dialog-frame.mjs`, `offline/tool-job.mjs` and `offline/design-values.mjs` hold them.

**WHEN IT ASKS, AND ONE ASSIGNMENT SPELLED ONCE.** A check-out records who the tool went to, so it always opens the dialog (1c); a check-in asks only a person on several jobs (1e) and records on the press for one on a single job (1d), as every transition did before. `asksBeforeRecording` is the split. "One assignment is used without asking" has three readers — the registration's starting job, the transition's, and the press — so it is `lib/toolJob.js:onlyJob`, and `offline/tool-job.mjs` fails any other file on the axis that counts a job list for one. The press and the opener are one button, whose word is the event's.

**A CHECK-IN ON SEVERAL JOBS STARTS WITH NONE CHOSEN, NOT ON THE TOOL'S OWN JOB.** The person is asked because the app does not know which site they are at, and choosing the tool item's last job for them would record a site they did not pick. That is the same kind of error as #363's and not the same error: there the retirement asked the job again and recorded a site the tool had never been at; here nothing is asked twice, but a value the app supplied would be recorded as the reader's answer. The commitment waits for a choice (0l). **#463's foot bar starts the same way and does not wait**: its press acts, and a press with no job chosen is answered under the pill (1g).

**WHAT A SUBMISSION MAY BE IS STILL ONE FUNCTION.** The dialog asks `readSubmission` of what it would send and sends only what it takes; the action asks again. The commitment cannot act while that answer refuses — a check-out's two answers can be missing but never wrong, so the act "cannot act yet" (0f) and the empty field is the reason, with no line before it. The form's own `name.trim()` guard went with the form, and `Enter a name to check out.` (1g) is the action's answer to a caller with no screen. **Since #463 it is the phone's too**: the foot bar's press acts whatever is missing and says this under the name field, as 1g draws it, reading `fieldRefusals`, which `readSubmission` reads.

**THE RETIREMENT IS 0l's CONFIRM.** Its title asks with the tool's name and the line under it is the id — 0l's Confirm and Tools 0a's sheet that confirms say the same words at both widths, so one component draws both — and its sentence is the design's. The commitment is `Retire tool`, filled red, beside `Cancel`.

**TWO WIDTHS, AND 1f's THREE SHEETS ARE PARTS.** At a desk the job is 0a's choice and the name the registration's combobox (1j), its list headed `Recently at this job`, the name sheet's words, so the two widths say one thing. Below the phone's edge each dialog opens as Tools 0a's sheet, and the job and the name are fields that open 1f's job sheet and name sheet. **1f draws the check-out on a phone as a foot bar** — the job, the name and the button at the page's foot — and the foot bar is 0a's, which #473 builds for the sign-in screen's steps; so the tool item page's foot bar is #463's, built on #473's, and drawing it here would have made two of one part. Until then the dialog opens as a sheet, and the job sheet, the name sheet and the confirm sheet are built as 1f draws them, to open from the foot bar as they are. **#463 built the foot bar on `app/components/BottomBar.js`, and they open from it as they are.**

**WHAT A LANDING DOES, MEASURED.** Next 16.2.10 rejects an action's promise with its redirect so that `RedirectBoundary` remounts the tree under the component that called it — `server-action-reducer.js` and `redirect-boundary.js` say so in as many words — and eight landings here showed it: the opener and the heading were new nodes after each, focus on the heading after a dialog's and on the document after the press's. The retirement's left the heading the same node, because its question left the page with the offer and nothing threw. **So no refusal can stand beside a success**, and the `superseded` state an earlier pass of this issue wrote on the reasoning that one could went before it was committed. What is left for the page to keep is a refusal's, which re-renders in place (#378): the dialog's opening ends with the event it was for, so two refusals flipping the status out and back cannot reopen it, and a dialog taken off the page while open hands focus to its opener while the page still has it and to the heading otherwise — the frame's rule, since the browser restores focus only when asked to close. **#456's notes above record a registration's landing that kept the dialog's state**, on a landing that moved the query; that landing was not walked here, and the two are not reconciled.

**THE REFUSAL IN PLACE, MEASURED.** With a check-out open on 26-DEMO-02, `soo@`'s assignments were narrowed to 26-DEMO-01 and the press refused in 3 operations, writing nothing. The dialog stood open, the same node, with `Choose a job you are assigned to.` above its actions, the typed name kept, the job back at `Choose a job` and the commitment unable to act until 26-DEMO-01 was chosen. **Focus fell to the document** as the commitment was disabled under the press, and nothing brought it back when the refusal landed; that was the frame's `busy`, which #469 replaced with a submitting state (`A dialog that submits (#469)`).

**WHAT A BROWSER SHOWED**, as `soo@` on `HYE-TL-261001-022`:

- **At 1440**: the check-out dialog 420 wide with the caret in the name; the job's list and the name's at the field's width, the name's headed `Recently at this job` and holding `Sam Ortiz` once that job had handed to him; the move notice under the job when the other was chosen. Enter on the opener opened it; Escape shut the list first and the dialog next; Cancel and Escape handed focus back to the opener. The check-in dialog on two jobs opened with none chosen and its commitment unable to act. The Confirm said `Retire DEMO Rotary Hammer?` with the id at 13 in the id face, its sentence named as the dialog's description and `Retire tool` in Red, and Escape handed focus back.
- **At 375**: each dialog a sheet 375 wide at the foot with its top corners 28 and the handle, the title 17 at 600, fields 50 under labels at 15, the actions 48 and stacked with the commitment over `Cancel`. The job sheet's rows 56 with the Inner rule, a row choosing and putting the sheet away. The name sheet with `Done` 48 at its head's end, the filled field with an Accent caret and its clear mark, the job's names under `Recently at this job`; a name picked or typed, `Done` and the keyboard's done key putting it away. With one job the job is stated, with no sheet to open. The confirm sheet with its title 24 from the top, the id under it at 15 in Ink 3, `Retire tool` over `Cancel` and focus on `Cancel`. A press behind the job sheet, the name sheet or the confirm sheet closed it and handed focus back; behind the check-out's sheet, or in its room at the foot, nothing happened. No sideways scroll.
- **The name sheet drew `Recently at this job` over no rows** once a typed name matched none of them, where the desk's list closes. The head now goes with its rows.

**FOUND BY READING BEFORE THE WALK.** On one job the dialog's `openedFor` was never reset, so the check-out offered again after a press would have opened with nobody asking, and a dialog taken off the page while open sent focus to the heading with its opener still there. The walk then showed that a landing remounts the tree, which leaves the first reachable only by two refusals in a row; both fixes stand, and each is a check.

**THE BASE GAINED NINE `Tool Log` ROWS UNDER `HYE-TL-261001-022`, WHICH IS RETIRED, KEPT**: `-002` to `-010` — four `Checked Out`, to `Sam Ortiz` on 26-DEMO-02, `Dana K`, `Sam Ortiz` and `Lee Park` on 26-DEMO-01; four `Checked In`; and one `Retired` on 26-DEMO-01 — with the nine cache writes that followed them. `soo@`'s `Assigned Jobs` was narrowed to 26-DEMO-01 for the one-job walk, put back to its two in their order and read back. Nothing was deleted. Each event cost 7 operations and the refusal 3, #362's and #363's figures; the page 6, or 7 where it offers a check-out. Another client on the dev server re-rendered the page on every save while the pane was parked, 22 renders and 132 reads.

**WHAT THE CHECKS HOLD.**

- `offline/dialog-frame.mjs`: no file under `app/(tools)/` imports the old frame, and the eight that call the frame are the dialogs drawn on it; the sheet's layout, its mark and which kind; a press behind it; the Confirm's line; `done`; the list's head; the controls' sizes in a sheet, which since #473 reach no other control but a sign-in page's, and a busy action's fill, which no hover or press changes; and where focus goes when a dialog is taken away open.
- `offline/tool-transition.mjs`: when it asks, what the dialog asks `readSubmission` and when its commitment acts, the opening and its end, where a refusal stands, both widths' controls on one state, the Confirm, and the two sheets as parts.
- `offline/tool-job.mjs`: `onlyJob` by value, and no other file on the axis counting a job list for one.
- `offline/design-values.mjs`: #458 out of the waiting list, each of its names read, handed on or undeclared (`design-system.md`).

**146 mutations were run, and every one fails the tier; each of the 145 new assertions fails under at least one.** Two of the first set survived, and each was a check's: offering the cut on what typing narrowed passed because only three of the five names carried the fragment the check typed, so a sixth went in; and the retirement's way out spelled in another word passed because the check read only the copy's value, so the buttons are read too.

## A dialog that submits (#469)

The frame hands its `busy` to everything it holds: the form's submit draws 0f's Working and every other control locks, none of them disabled, and while a dialog stays open the frame puts back focus it let fall. `FormBusy` in `app/components/Controls.js` carries the state from `app/components/DialogFrame.js` to the controls, `lib/controls.js:buttonBusyState` says what a button is under it and `lib/dialogFrame.js:focusLost` whether focus fell. The registration's commitment names what it will create by its count, the transition's and the retirement's say their `-ing` words, and a job taken away under the transition's open dialog takes the opening's start again. `offline/dialog-frame.mjs`, `offline/tool-registration.mjs`, `offline/tool-transition.mjs` and `offline/tool-screen-words.mjs` hold them.

**THREE DIALOGS SUBMIT, AND THE CHECK FINDS THEM RATHER THAN LISTING THEM.** The registration — opened from `/tools`, from a tool's page and from the offer's `Create the rest` — the transition, for a check-out and for a check-in on several jobs, and the retirement. The landing's two answer by editing the address, the labels' read is made by their opener before the dialog opens and their print sends nothing, and 1f's two sheets choose and type. `offline/dialog-frame.mjs` walks every component on the axis that draws the frame, holds each that submits to handing it `busy` and its commitment a word with no button disabled for the sending, and each that does not to handing it no `busy` — so a fourth dialog that submits is held the day it is drawn. The check-in on one job, which records on its press with no dialog, keeps `disabled={pending}`: it is not a dialog's action. **#463 gave it 0f's Working instead** — `busy` and the event's `-ing` word — so the press keeps focus while it sends, and the check finds it with the foot bar's form.

**THE FRAME HOLDS THE STATE BECAUSE IT ALREADY DID.** It has taken `busy` since #456 so as not to close while a submission is in flight, and handing that one value on through a context is what makes "the frame gives that state to every dialog that submits" true without each dialog wiring each control. Props per dialog were the other shape, and a control one dialog forgot would have locked nothing with no check able to see it; the frame drawing the commitment itself was the third, and would have taken the red variant, the reason a commitment cannot act and its word into the frame. The sign-in steps keep their own props, since they stand in no frame, and the button reads both.

**NOTHING IS DISABLED WHILE A DIALOG SENDS, AND THAT IS ITS FOCUS RULE.** Measured on a page of this repository's browser with nothing else on it: a focused button disabled gives focus up to the document after the next style update, with no `blur` and no `focusout`, and one taken off the page gives it up at once, with none either — which is what #458 saw when the commitment was disabled under the press. So the working submit is `aria-busy` and `aria-disabled` and turns its press away, every other button is `aria-disabled` and turns its press away, the fields are read-only — the choice `aria-readonly` and deaf to its keys and its press, the typed value with its list kept shut — and the phone's field opens no sheet. Each keeps its look, as 1j's Creating draws them, and takes no hover. Focus stays where the submission found it: on the commitment that was pressed, or in the field Enter was pressed in. **Enter while it sends sends nothing**: implicit submission is a press on the form's submit, which the working submit turns away, so the frontend guard CLAUDE.md asks for against a double write is in one place.

**FOCUS THAT FALLS IN A DIALOG LEFT OPEN COMES BACK, AND A REFUSAL IS WHEN IT FALLS.** The registration's and the retirement's commitments can act again the moment a refusal lands, so focus stays on them. The transition's cannot once the job it was pressed with left the reader's assignments and several remain, since none is chosen then. The browser says nothing as that happens, so while a dialog is open the frame is told of what can take focus away — a control disabled, hidden or made inert, or taken off the page — follows what holds focus through `focusin` and, in a document with no focus to fire one, through what is focused at each change, and asks `focusLost` each time. Fallen focus goes to the first thing the body holds that can take it, where the dialog asks, or to the title; focus in a sheet opened over the dialog went there on purpose and is left alone. **Not watched, and written in the frame's header**: an element hidden by its class or by the screen's width, which is not a change the observer is told of and which nothing on this axis does to what holds focus.

**THE COUNT IS `readQuantity`'s, AND SO IS ITS ABSENCE.** 1j's own script names the commitment with its count while that count is a whole number from 1 to 100 and says `Create tools` otherwise, and that is `readQuantity`'s verdict, so the dialog asks it of the field as typed: the words never name a count the press would refuse, and an empty field, a 0 or a 140 says the act alone, as 1j's Refused draws it under `Max 100 at a time.`. **The 124 the drawing held the commitment at was no name**: it was 0f's minimum width for `Creating…` — 16, 8, the word and 32, measured at 123.77 — which #473's one cell for both labels reached, and since #495 a busy action keeps its resting width with the spinner alone in it, so nothing holds one (`design-system.md`).

**THE `-ing` WORDS THE DESIGN DOES NOT DRAW ARE 0f's RULE APPLIED.** 1c, 1e and 1f draw no submitting state for the tool item page's dialogs, and the mock's commitments act without one. So `Checking out…` and `Checking in…` — the particle kept, since `Checking…` would not say which way the tool is going — and `Retiring…`, the verb without its object, as 0f's `no count` keeps every working label short. The same rule widens those commitments at rest to the working word in their padding. **In a sheet it is Tools 0a's Busy**, the phone's spinner alone and the word kept for assistive tech, which reverses #473's reading; `design-system.md` has why.

**A JOB TAKEN AWAY UNDER AN OPEN DIALOG TAKES THE OPENING'S START AGAIN, AND THE PHONE IS WHERE IT STUCK.** Reproduced at 375 before the fix: a check-out sheet opened on two jobs with 26-DEMO-02 chosen, `soo@` narrowed to 26-DEMO-01, the press refused — and the job a stated field saying `Choose a job` that nothing could open, over a commitment that could not act; only `Cancel` left. The dialog kept its job id across #378's re-render in place while the plan came back with one job, so nothing was chosen, and with one job the field took no sheet and the job sheet was not drawn. At a desk the choice still opened, so there it was a slower path rather than a dead end. The cause was the one the walk was set up to test, so the dialog takes the opening's start again — `onlyJob`, the one job left chosen or none of several — whenever the plan stops holding the chosen job, inside `TransitionDialog.js`.

**WHAT A BROWSER SHOWED**, as `soo@` on the dev server already running in this checkout (`next dev -p 3100`), at 1440 and 375. The pane was hidden and drew no frames, so the 300 ms switch was read off the transitions it starts and its end by finishing them, and each Server Action's request was held 1.5 to 4 s inside the page to read the wait:

- **The registration's words**: `Create 1 tool` 123.78 wide, the same to `Create 9 tools`, `Create 10 tools` 129.56 and `Create 100 tools` 139.20, `Cancel` moving only with them; 140, nothing and 0 said `Create tools`.
- **A registration sending**, 55 ms after the press: `aria-busy`, the fill and the width kept, focus on the commitment, the resting word still drawn on a 300 ms visibility transition; `Cancel`, the close, the count, its steps and the job locked and none disabled; `Cancel`, Escape and the close each left it open. Settled, `Creating…` beside a 16 spinner, at 1440 and in the 319 dialog at 375. Each landed on its tool's page with what it wrote selected and focus on the heading.
- **Refusals that leave the dialog open**, with the dialogs opened on 26-DEMO-02 before `soo@` was narrowed: the registration's at both widths kept focus on a commitment that could act, the refusal under the job. The check-out's, before the fix, put the job back to `Choose a job` at 1440 with the commitment unable to act and focus moved to the job's choice — the frame's rule seen working on the case it exists for — and at 375 left the stuck state above, focus moved to the name's field.
- **A refusal that closes it**: a check-out pressed on a page somebody else had moved closed the dialog with `Nothing was recorded. Somebody else scanned this first.` and focus on the same control, `Check in` by then.
- **Sending in a sheet**: a 20 spinner centered in the 48 commitment, the word announced and not drawn, `Cancel` locked without dimming; a press behind the sheet that confirms left it open.
- **The rest**: a check-out, a check-in and a retirement at each width, each with its word and its width kept and landing with focus on the heading; a retirement pressed after the other tab's took the dialog away with both controls and left focus on the heading. Nothing ran off the side at 375.

**THE FIX WAS SEEN IN A SECOND NARROWING, ON PAGES LOADED AFTER IT.** It was written once the stuck state was seen, inside the first narrowing, and the pages already open kept running the code they had loaded: in a pane that draws no frames the edit's hot update never reached them while its server render did — two dialogs that had not been pressed went to `Choose a job` from that render alone. A dialog opened on the narrowed reader has one job from the start, so the fix needed the reader narrowed again. With two pages loaded afresh, each check-out opened on two jobs with none chosen, 26-DEMO-02 chosen and a name typed, `soo@` narrowed and each pressed:

- **At 1440** the refused dialog was the same node, its job back to 26-DEMO-01 with the job's one option chosen and the hidden field carrying its id, the name kept and the commitment able to act.
- **At 375** the job was the stated field saying 26-DEMO-01 — the one-job field 1f draws — the name kept, the commitment able to act and focus still on it, read before anything else was touched.

Each refusal was 3 operations and wrote nothing, and nothing was pressed a second time. `offline/tool-transition.mjs` holds the re-start off the source.

**THE FIGURES, ON A DEV SERVER AGAINST THE LIVE BASE.** `registerToolItemsAction` is 6 operations for one tool item and 2 for a refusal of the job; `recordToolItemEventAction` and `retireToolItemAction` are 7 for an event and 3 for a refusal — #470's, #362's and #363's figures, since this issue adds no read and no write. A tool's page is 4 and a tool item's 6, or 7 where it offers a check-out. Another client connected to the dev server re-rendered a tool item page on every save, as #458 found: 183 renders and 1,106 reads followed the 38 saves this issue made, a few of them this walk's own open tabs.

**THE BASE GAINED TWO TOOL ITEMS AND EIGHT `Tool Log` ROWS, KEPT**: `HYE-TL-261002-001` and `-002` under `DEMO Rotary Hammer` on 26-DEMO-01, each `Created`, `Checked Out` — to `Dana K` and `Lee Park` — `Checked In` and `Retired`, all on 26-DEMO-01, with the six cache writes that followed the events. `soo@`'s `Assigned Jobs` was narrowed to 26-DEMO-01 twice — for the refusals, and to see the fix — and each time put back to its two in their order and read back. No `Tools` row was written, and nothing was deleted.

**WHAT THE CHECKS HOLD.**

- `offline/dialog-frame.mjs`: `buttonBusyState` and `focusLost` by value; that the frame hands its `busy` on, locks its close, and keeps focus as above, and each control reads the state and locks as its own kind does; the sheet's Busy; and the census of the axis's dialogs that submit — each beside a planted file doing it wrong.
- `offline/tool-registration.mjs`: the submit's words by value, through `readQuantity` at its edges, and the commitment off the dialog with no button disabled for the sending.
- `offline/tool-transition.mjs`: the working words by value and for every event a status offers, both commitments' working words and their `disabled`, and the job's re-start.
- `offline/tool-screen-words.mjs`: the collector reading the count's words and the working word.

**A hundred and one mutations were run and all hundred and one fail the tier, and each of the 81 assertions this issue added or changed fails under at least one of them** — among the mutations every reader the checks added, so each planted copy's assertion is seen to fail too. The first run was 82 and left twenty assertions no mutation reached: both ends of `buttonBusyState`, a red commitment's hover, a working word for an event no status offers, a dialog that asks nothing handed `busy`, the collector reading the one-tool words as a literal, and the planted copies' readers; each has a mutation aimed at it now. The one label not counted is `the constant yielded N strings`, an existing assertion whose label prints the count this issue's three strings moved.

## The design's words (#455)

The tools screens say what the design says. The act is `create` — `New tools` opens it, `Create tools` submits it and `Create the rest` opens it again, and a tool's own page said `Create more of this tool` until #456 gave it `New tools` too — a `Tool Items` row is a `tool` in every sentence about one and an `item` where its tool's own page counts or heads them, and the log's first event is `Created`. The words are `TOOL_REGISTRATION_COPY`, `TOOL_LIST_COPY`, `TOOL_ITEM_COPY`, `TOOL_TRANSITION_COPY` and `TOOL_LABEL_PAGE_COPY`; `offline/tool-screen-words.mjs` holds the verb across the axis. **#485 made the act `add`** — `Add tools` opens and titles it, `Add 5 tools` submits it and `Add 2 more` opens it again — and `The act says add (#485)` below has why, and what still says `create`.

**THE EVENT IS A VALUE ON THE BASE, SO THE WORD HAD TO REACH THE BASE.** The history prints each row's stored `Event` — `logRowFacts` hands it over verbatim, as every screen in this app prints a select value — so the screen word for it is the option's name, and a map from one to the other would give one event two spellings. The option was renamed in the Airtable UI, in place: its choice id is unchanged, so the 33 rows that held `Registered` read `Created` with no row written. **Adding `Created` and deleting `Registered`, which is how this issue was first written, would have emptied those 33 cells** — Airtable clears every cell holding a deleted choice — leaving the first entry of 33 histories with no event and a repair only by rewriting an append-only log. `airtable-access.md` records the rule, and why it was established from Airtable's reference rather than by deleting anything. **The design's `Checked out` and `Checked in` were not taken**: the base's options are title case, `offline/tool-status.mjs` holds that, and the history reads `Checked Out` as every screen reads `In Review`; sentence-cased select values would be a decision for the whole app, and the brief says so to the design. **#463 took them, and made it a decision per axis instead**: `Checked Out`, `Checked In` and `In Stock` were renamed in place to `Checked out`, `Checked in` and `In stock` when the design settled the tools screens' words, the materials axis keeps its title case, and `naming.md` holds the rule.

**THE KEY FOLLOWED THE VALUE, AND THE WRITER NOW REFUSES WHAT A MISSED KEY WOULD SEND.** `TOOL_EVENT.REGISTERED` became `TOOL_EVENT.CREATED`, because a key holding another word is the false identifier the vocabulary module exists against. A call site left on the old key reads `undefined`, and `undefined` drops out of the request body — the no-typecast refusal the writer relied on never fires, and the row lands with no `Event`. `createToolLogEntry` refuses an event outside the vocabulary beside its job and recorder guards, `offline/tool-transition.mjs` pins the guard, and `offline/tool-registration.mjs` reads the registration's own `event:` argument off the AST.

**THE IDENTIFIERS KEEP `register`, AND A CHECK IS WHAT MAKES THAT SAFE.** `create` is the prefix of every single-table writer in `lib/airtable/`, the two this act calls among them, so `createToolItemsAction` would read as a wrapper of `createToolItems` while it writes three tables; half the rest were the form's route, which #456 removed; and `MAX_TOOL_ITEMS_PER_REGISTRATION` reaches the label screen — the labels' read since #457. The hazard of a divergence like that is #227's — an identifier teaches its word to the next string written beside it — so `offline/tool-screen-words.mjs` fails any string a tools screen renders that says `register`, over the routes `TOOLS_ROUTES` declares and typed out beside them. `naming.md` holds the row.

**THE NOUN IS THE DESIGN'S, AND IT REVERSED TWO RULES THIS AXIS HELD.** `Tools` owns `tool`, so a tool item was never "a tool" (#338); and a tool item was never a bare `item` (#334's reading of #303). The design says `tool` for the object in every sentence and `items` on its tool's page. The first is a deliberate divergence with an owner — `correction`'s shape in `naming.md` — kept honest by restructuring the few sentences that meet the kind and its units at once (`These join the ones already under it`, `Creating writes the tool before its items`, `Nothing was named to print`), so one word never names two rows in one sentence. The second was stricter than #303 itself, which drops the modifier where no second kind of item row is on the string, and no tools screen shows one. The four scanners that failed a bare `item` went, and each constant holds the inverse: it no longer says `tool item`. **The one string that still does is `Tool item not found`.** The design's replacement adds `It may have been deleted.`, which names the least likely way a code reaches that screen — the app cannot delete a tool item — and points away from the likely one, a code misread off a worn label; the design has been handed the four real causes. `offline/tool-item-view.mjs` pins the heading as the one remainder, so its rewrite fails that check until the list is empty. **#463 rewrote it as `Tool not found`**, so no tools screen says `tool item` now and that check holds no remainder.

**TWO OF THE DESIGN'S WORDS NEEDED MORE THAN WORDS.** `3 of 5 tools created` needs how many were asked for, which the landing did not carry and the page may not count off the selection — it never reads `id`, for #443's reason — so `asked` rides beside `unwritten` and the pair is read as one: `1 ≤ unwritten < asked ≤ 100`, or no fork. `Got it` is a control the notice did not have: #449 kept it without one because nothing repairs what it names, and the design gave it a dismissal that repairs nothing either, so what #449 said a design may not lose — the fork is a question and the notice a fact — is what remains. `Got it` deletes `unlogged` from the current address the way `Not now` deletes the fork's two keys, and neither touches the other's.

**ONE SENTENCE WAS NOT TAKEN.** `Join a job to create tools` tells the reader to do what no screen here lets them do — the office assigns jobs in Airtable — so the sentence keeps `Ask for a job assignment first.`, and the design has the reason. **The design answered in #456** with `Ask the office to assign you to a job`, said before every opener drawn disabled for such a reader. The rest of the design's words went in as written; where the design drew nothing, the sweep carried the verb and the noun into the sentence that was there. **1k's two dialog sentences are longer than what went in**, and `The account in dialogs (#459)` records which of their words #459 took and which it left out.

**ONE OF THE DESIGN'S WORDS REACHES NO SCREEN.** `QR label for this tool` went into `TOOL_ITEM_COPY.symbolAlt`, and the tool item page read in a browser draws no `alt` and no accessible name for its symbol at all — the paragraph above that called the `alt` the failure state is corrected where it stands. The word stays because the design named it; whether the symbol carries it is a decision about the page, and the brief says so rather than this issue making it. Read after the rename with `soo@` at 1440 and at 375, the first history entry of `HYE-TL-260909-004` and of `-007`, written on 9/9 as `Registered`, reads `Created`.

**WHAT THE CHECKS HOLD.** `offline/tool-screen-words.mjs` fails `register` in any string a tools screen renders; `offline/tool-registration.mjs` holds the account's pair as literals either side of each bound and reads the action's `asked` and `event` arguments off its AST; `offline/tool-list-view.mjs` holds that each dismissal deletes exactly its own keys and that the page reads `asked`; `offline/tool-transition.mjs` pins the writer's event guard and where its list comes from; and `offline/tool-status.mjs` holds the vocabulary and the creation script's options, in order, against each other. **Thirty-five mutations were run and all thirty-five fail the tier** — twenty-two against the words, the fork's pair and the dismissals, and thirteen against the rename once the base had it: the value, the script's option, the action left on the old key or spelling the value, the fixture, and the one string left for the design.

**THREE OF THE BASE'S DESCRIPTIONS WERE FALSE BEFORE THE RENAME AND IT MADE TWO MORE, AND THE SCRIPT HAD FALLEN BEHIND THE BASE.** The `Tools` table named a `createToolAction` that does not exist — `upsertTool` finds or creates the kind under a lock; `Tool Name` said Airtable's `=` on text "is" case-insensitive, which #338 measured it is NOT, which is why the match goes through `LOWER(TRIM(...))`; `Status` sent the reason for a retirement to `Tool Log."Notes"`, which #363 deleted; and `Checked Out To` named `Registered` among the events it is blank on, which the rename made false along with `Event`'s own text. All five were PATCHed from `scripts/import/create_tools_334.py`, whose own copy still carried `Notes`, lacked `Checked Out To` (#376) and held both `Job` descriptions as they were before #363 rewrote them on the base. Read back afterwards, every table and field description and both selects' choices match the script's with no difference, and its `--dry-run` finds nothing to do.

### The act says add (#485)

Putting tools into stock is `add` on the screen: `Add tools` on each opener that begins a registration and as the dialog's title, `Add 1 tool` and `Add 5 tools` on its commitment and `Add tools` over a count it would refuse, `Adding…` while it sends, `Couldn't add the tools. Try again.` when nothing was written, and the fork's `3 of 5 tools added`, `2 couldn't be added.` and `Add 2 more`. Only the words the issue names moved, and 1k is drawn as it was — the title, the tool's name, one sentence, `Not now` and the answer that goes on. The words are `TOOL_REGISTRATION_COPY`'s; `offline/tool-registration.mjs`, `offline/tool-list-view.mjs` and `offline/tool-screen-words.mjs` hold them.

**`create` READ AS A NEW KIND, AND MOST REGISTRATIONS ARE NOT ONE.** In this app `create` makes a record that did not exist — `Create Invoice`, `Create Address`, `Create Category` — and a registration is usually more of a tool the company already has, written under the tool its name finds, so `Create 5 tools` read as five new kinds. `add` is the verb whether the name typed is a tool the company has or a new one, because the line under the name already says which (`Adds to …`, `Creates a new tool`) and the commitment does not have to.

**WHAT STILL SAYS `create` NAMES SOMETHING ELSE, AND ONE SENTENCE WAS LEFT.** `Creates a new tool` says the name typed coins a `Tools` row, which is what the act does on its way rather than the act. The creation date, in the notice's title and sentence, is the moment the log's first row records, and that row's `Event` stays `Created`: a base value every history prints, renamed in place once (#455) and asked to move by nothing here. The empty tool's `If you were creating some, it stopped before any were saved.` was about the act and not among the words #485 changed, so it was the design's to reword, and #495 took the design's `adding` for it. `offline/tool-screen-words.mjs` names each with why, and fails any other string a tools screen renders that says `create` as well as an entry no screen renders any more — so the list shrinks as the design rewords, the shape `offline/tool-item-view.mjs` kept for `tool item` until #463.

**THE FORK'S ANSWER NAMES HOW MANY ARE LEFT, AND IT IS THE NUMBER THE DIALOG OPENS AT.** `Add 2 more` is handed `account.unwritten` — the count the landing's address carries as not written, `1 ≤ unwritten < asked ≤ 100` (`readRegistrationAccount`) — and so is the registration dialog it opens, as the count it starts at since #459. One value for both, so the words and the dialog cannot name two numbers, and the dialog's commitment then names the same one, `Add 2 tools`. `Add 1 more` at one: `more` takes no plural, so the builder has no singular branch where `submit` has one. It is the count #449's `Register the other N` carried and #455's `Create the rest` dropped.

**THE CODE KEEPS `register`.** The screen's word moved twice and `register` names the act across its three tables; `naming.md` holds the row.

**WHAT A BROWSER SHOWED**, as `soo@` on the dev server already running in this checkout (`next dev -p 3100`, which this issue did not start), in the Browser pane at 1440 × 900 while the pane was hidden, so read off the DOM with its transitions finished:

- **The openers**: `/tools`' and `DEMO Rotary Hammer`'s page's each said `Add tools` and opened the dialog titled `Add tools`, with the intro under it on the list and the tool's name on the tool's page.
- **The commitment**: `Add 1 tool`, `Add 5 tools`, `Add 9 tools`, `Add 10 tools` and `Add 100 tools` for those counts, and `Add tools` for 140, 0 and nothing.
- **The fork**, from a landing typed by hand as #459's were: at `asked=5&unwritten=2`, `3 of 5 tools added`, the tool's name, `2 couldn't be added.`, `Not now` and `Add 2 more`; `Add 2 more` shut it and opened the dialog on the tool at 2, saying `Add 2 tools`, and that dialog's Cancel brought the fork back; at `unwritten=1`, `4 of 5 tools added`, `1 couldn't be added.` and `Add 1 more`; `Not now` took both keys out of the address and left focus on the heading.
- **One registration**: `DEMO Cordless Drill` typed on `/tools`, which said `Adds to DEMO Cordless Drill, which already has 1 item` under it, 1 on 26-DEMO-01. Its Server Action was held 3 s inside the page: the commitment was `aria-busy` and not disabled, kept focus, and drew the spinner and `Adding…` where `Add 1 tool` had stood, with every other button `aria-disabled`. It landed on the tool's page with `HYE-TL-261005-001` first and selected, `In stock` on 26-DEMO-01, `2 items`, no dialog and focus on the heading, and that tool item's history opens on `Created`.

**Not seen**: `Couldn't add the tools. Try again.`, which takes a registration that writes nothing; the second opener an empty list draws, since every tool on this base has items; and a fork a failed registration really produced. `offline/tool-registration.mjs` runs the last against a fake base and holds the whole fork it leaves on the landing, and `offline/tool-list-view.mjs` reads the fork's expressions off its source.

**THE FIGURES.** `registerToolItemsAction` was 6 operations, as #469 measured; the tool's page 4 and `/tools` 5. The changes reached this checkout as copies from a mirror the mutations ran in, and another client connected to the dev server re-rendered a tool item page nine times on the first, 54 reads. **The base gained one tool item and its `Created` row, kept**: `HYE-TL-261005-001` under `DEMO Cordless Drill` on 26-DEMO-01. No `Tools` row was written.

**WHAT THE CHECKS HOLD.**

- `offline/tool-registration.mjs`: each changed word by value; the fork's answer at two and at one; over every count a shortfall can carry, that the answer, the count the dialog opens at and its commitment name one number; and the whole fork a failed batch leaves.
- `offline/tool-list-view.mjs`: that the fork hands its answer and its form one expression, and its title's and its sentence's expressions, each beside a planted fork doing it wrong.
- `offline/tool-screen-words.mjs`: no `register` and no unnamed `create` on any tools screen, no stale entry, and both matchers and both readers on planted strings.

**Thirty-three mutations were run and all thirty-three fail the tier, and each of the 53 assertions this issue added or changed fails under at least one.** The first run was 31 and two survived, both the new check's own: the reader of unnamed strings and the reader of stale entries were reached by no planted string, so each became a function the planted cases call. The one label not counted is `the constant yielded N strings`, an existing assertion whose label prints the count the fork's answer moved.

## The design's values on this axis (#462)

`app/designValues.css` declares the values the design settled for these screens, each under a conventional name and in rem, and `design-system.md` carries the reasoning, the names, the table from the design's words to them and what waits. The registration dialog's frame and controls were the first readers (#456); the issues that apply the rest come next, and what each owes is this.

**A TOOLS SCREEN READS THE NAME AND WRITES NO VALUE.** A value the drawings carry that the spec gives no name to — `design-system.md` lists several, a row's 16 checkbox among them — is named in the same commit as the first file that reads it, and a name the check's table marks for an issue is unmarked by the pull request that reads it.

**WHO MAY READ ONE IS WHO CALLS THE FILE, NOT WHERE IT SITS.** A component may live anywhere and read a name as long as nothing outside `app/(tools)/` calls it — or, since #473, outside it and the sign-in steps — which is what lets #460's rail sit where any screen could call it while only this layout does. A component this axis shares with the materials screens reads none, so a screen here that wants one of its shapes draws its own; the two dialogs on the tool item page importing `MODAL_BACKDROP` and `MODAL_CARD` are that case.

**THE PHONE'S VALUES ARE NAMES OF THEIR OWN**, under `mobile-` and read under Tailwind's `max-sm` variant beside the web name, and **THE FIRST ISSUE TO READ A FACE LOADS IT**, into the variable the face resolves to, in a module only the design's screens reach — the arrangement the label page already has with Inconsolata, which no name here is for. **The faces load in `app/faces/`, a module a face, since #473**, and this layout imports all three; they loaded in the layout itself from #456 until the sign-in steps' layout came to draw two of them (`design-system.md`). `offline/design-values.mjs` fails each of these.

## The navigation (#460)

The tools screens carry the frame Claude Design draws every screen in (0m): a rail of the app's sections beside a column the screen scrolls in, and a breadcrumb above a record's title. `app/components/Rail.js` is the rail and the column, `app/components/Breadcrumb.js` the bar, and `lib/navigation.js` the sections, which one a screen is in and every word the two say. The design was read from the files as Design sent them a second time on 2026-10-01 — `design-system.md` says which answers they carry — and they are what "the files of 2026-10-01" means below.

**THE RAIL IS WRITTEN FOR EVERY SCREEN AND CALLED BY ONE LAYOUT, AND #258 IS WHAT CHANGES THAT.** The screens above this axis hold twenty-four width containers across twenty pages (`Where the screens stand`), and a rail beside them would fight each one, so the materials sections are on the rail and their screens are not under it: pressing one leaves the rail behind, which is the state until #258 gathers those screens into one layout. That commit moves the call from `app/(tools)/layout.js` to `app/layout.js`, moves the three faces with it and widens `offline/design-values.mjs`' boundary to every route file. **Nothing in the rail changes then, because it reads its section off the address** rather than taking it from its caller, and a root layout cannot know which page is below it.

**SIX SECTIONS, AND THE DESIGN'S SEVENTH WAITS FOR A SCREEN.** The design draws seven, `Office` last. `app/admin/` holds four create forms, no page listing them and no link to any of them, so `Office` would open nothing; the issue that gives the office a screen adds a line to `NAVIGATION_SECTIONS` and one to the check's table. The words are the design's, in sentence case, so `Purchase requests` and `Purchase orders` disagree with those lists' headings — `docs/briefs/_shared.md` records it beside the root screen's own — and `Tools` is `TOOL_LIST_COPY.heading` imported, so the section and the list's heading are one string.

**WHICH SECTION A SCREEN IS IN IS READ OFF ITS ADDRESS.** A section owns the first segments of its routes; Tools owns two — `tools` and `tool-items`, read off `TOOLS_ROUTES` — since a tool item is not under `/tools`, and owned `t` as well until #478 took the printed address out of the rail's layout. A section's link is `aria-current="page"` on its own list and `"true"` on a screen under it, and a route no section owns — `/`, the sign-in steps, `/addresses/new`, `/admin/*`, `/t/` — makes none current. **A dialog is not a route, which is what settles #457's labels**: the registration (#456) and the label dialog leave the address where the page that opened them put it, so the section stays that page's. Read in a browser, the registration dialog opens over the whole frame, the rail included, a press on the rail lands on the dialog, and `Escape` closes it back to `New tools` with the rail as it was. `offline/navigation.mjs` classifies every route the app serves in a literal table and compares it both ways, so a page added without a line there fails rather than falling into whichever section its first segment names, and a route that goes takes its line with it, as `/tool-items/labels`' did when #457 made it a dialog.

**THE EXPANDED RAIL STAYS EXPANDED ACROSS THE AXIS, AND NOTHING STORES IT.** The state is the rail's and the rail is the layout's, which Next does not render again on a navigation: expanded at 1440, it stayed expanded from `/tools` to a tool's page to a tool item's. A reload, a new tab, a scan and a trip through a screen without the rail start collapsed, which is the design's resting state. **Browser storage was weighed and refused**: the server cannot read it, so every load from 1280 up would draw the rail collapsed and push the screen 192px sideways after hydration. A cookie the layout read would avoid the shift, and it is more than one convenience is worth.

**FROM THE 1280 WINDOW THE RAIL PUSHES THE SCREEN; BELOW IT, IT COVERS IT AS A PANEL WITH NOTHING BEHIND IT.** The edge is Tailwind's `xl`, in the rail's classes and nowhere else: the handlers ask whether the rail is laid out over the column, so the breakpoint is written once. The files of 2026-10-01 took away the wash 0k drew behind a Panel, so a Panel is not modal — the screen beside it still answers — and it goes on a press outside it, on focus leaving it, on `Escape`, which hands focus back to the toggle, and on a section chosen from it. Measured expanded at 1281: in flow, 248 wide, no shadow, the column at 248. At 1279: laid over the column, 248 wide, the Panel's shadow at 0 12 48, the column still at 56 and nothing made inert. **From the keyboard alone at 1279**: `Enter` on the toggle opens it; `Tab` walks the six sections; one more `Tab` leaves the rail, closes it and lands on the breadcrumb's `Tools`; `Escape` closes it with focus on the toggle; a press on the screen closes it without navigating; and a section chosen from it navigates and closes it, `/tools` then marking `Tools` as the page.

**BELOW THE PHONE'S EDGE THERE IS NO RAIL.** The phone frame draws 1f's top bar in its place — the id and one 48 button — and no rail at any width, and a bar with room for one button has none for a rail's opener. So the two divide at `max-sm`, the edge every phone name divides at, and the top bar is #463's; until it lands, the breadcrumb is the way back on a tool's page and a tool item's at every width. Measured at 375: the rail is not drawn, the document scrolls rather than the column — 852 tall in 812 — the breadcrumb holds the top through a scroll, and nothing runs off the side.

**A COLLAPSED ICON'S TOOLTIP AND ITS NAME ARE ONE STRING.** A section's word is its link's `aria-label`, its text and the tooltip beside its icon, and the toggle's word is its name and its tooltip. The design differed once — a toggle named `Expand navigation` with an `Expand` tooltip — and the toggle says `Expand navigation` in both. The tooltip is 0k's: in the top layer and hidden from assistive tech, after 360ms and over the 120ms fade the drawings' stylesheet sets, on hover and on keyboard focus, and only while the rail is collapsed; `Escape` takes it away without moving focus. Measured against 0k: 10 beside the icon and centered on it to the pixel, 3, 9 and 4 inside, 13 on 20 at 500, white on Ink at a radius of 8. **The browser pane's own reading gave a link no name from text nested in a span** — the rail's links before their `aria-label`, and the breadcrumb's levels still — so that reading is not where a nested link's name is checked.

**THE ACCOUNT WAS NOT DRAWN HERE, AND THE SCAN'S HOP WAS WHY — #478 DREW IT** (`The account at the rail's foot`), with `/t/` out of the layout and the layout taking the page's read rather than making one. 0m draws an account at the rail's foot — an avatar, the name and a menu holding `Sign out` — and a tools screen had nowhere to sign out. The name needs the user: the session holds `{ userId }` alone, so even the part of the address before the domain is a `Users` read. **Measured with `getActiveUser` wrapped in React's `cache` and called by the layout**: the layout and the page share one read in a request — the tool item's page counted 5 in its own scope and the layout's 1 outside it, 6 as before — but the layout renders for `/t/[labelCode]` too, whose page reads nothing and redirects, so every scan gained a read, 0 to 1. And the layout's read lands outside every `withOpsLabel` scope, since a layout is no entry point. No added read on the hop was the condition, so the account and its two names went (`design-system.md`). A layout drawing it with no read seemed to need the address in the sealed session itself, which is `lib/session.js`'s and not this axis's; #478 found it needs only to take the page's.

**THE BREADCRUMB IS TWO SHAPES, AND IT READS NOTHING.** One level is a chevron and the level's name — on a tool's page, `Tools`, in place of the `← Tools` link the page carried (1b). More are a path split by `/` and ending on the screen's own name — on a tool item's page, `Tools / {tool} / {code}`, the code its label prints (1c). The tool is the row the page reads already, so the path costs nothing: `/tools/[toolRecordId]` is 4 operations and `/tool-items/[toolItemId]` 6, their recorded figures, and `/t/` 0. A screen saying a record is not there keeps its own way back and no breadcrumb (1l). Measured at 1440: 48 tall and sticky at the column's top, white at 0.82 over an 8 blur, a level at 13 on 20 and 600 in Ink 2, and the chevron's stroke 32 from the column's edge, on the Margin; the path's `/` is Ink 4 and its last level Ink at 600 with tabular figures, marked as the page.

**THE WORDMARK IS BRICOLAGE GROTESQUE, WHICH THE FILES OF 2026-10-01 CHANGED IT TO.** `HYE` at 700 in Ink and `USA Portal` at 500 in Ink 2, 16 on 24 at −0.03em, drawn only while the rail is expanded and kept until a collapsing rail has closed over it. The split is `lib/productName.js`'s `WORDMARK`, at the name's first space, since #473's sign-in wordmark became its second reader and the sign-in email's its third.

**THE FRAME NEVER SCROLLS; THE COLUMN DOES (0i).** From the phone's edge up the frame is the window's height and the column scrolls inside it, its scrollbar's lane reserved whether or not the bar shows and its thumb round with 1 of clearance, 2 until #495. How a column ends — 0i's End and its give-back — is the screen's, and #463's.

**THE RAIL NEVER PRINTS, AND #457'S PRINT MEETS NOTHING OF IT.** In print the rail is not drawn, and the frame and the column give up the window's height and their own scroll — a column held to the window would print one window of it. Neither sets a `display` in print, and at a label's 11 mm page `sm:` does not match, so their screen layout is not applied either: #457's print, which hides everything but the dialog and makes the dialog's ancestors `display: contents`, overrides nothing here. A `print:block` on the frame was taken out for that reason. The label screen printed headless in Chrome and Edge from a self-contained snapshot of the rendered page: two pages, each 10.922 x 12.023 mm, each symbol decoding at 1200 dpi as a version 2 to its own `/t/` address — the figures `A label is a page of its own` records. **#457 printed its dialog under this frame**, from a tool's page and from a tool item's, and `The labels as a dialog` has the figures.

**WHAT THE CHECKS HOLD.** `offline/navigation.mjs` holds the sections by value, every served route against its section, the one name per icon read off the rail's AST, the rail's one caller and the breadcrumb's two, and the edges: the rail undrawn below the phone's edge, three print rules and none a `display`, nothing stored and no breakpoint spelled. **Sixteen mutations were run and all sixteen fail it** — each of those undone, `Office` added, a section read by prefix, a word recased, the path ended on the record's id, and a page added with no line in the table. `offline/design-values.mjs` lost #460 from its waiting list, with 87 names read and 84 waiting, and `scripts/screen-strings.mjs` counts every layout above a screen rather than the root's alone, so the rail's words are on every tools screen's list; `/t/[labelCode]`, which renders nothing, carried them too until #478 took it out of the layout (`docs/briefs/strings/unreachable.md`).

## The account at the rail's foot (#478)

The rail draws 0m's account: who is reading, and a menu holding `Sign out`, the one way to sign out on a tools screen. `app/components/RailAccount.js` draws it, `lib/navigation.js:accountOf` says what it says, the tools layout hands it the user, and `/t/[labelCode]` left that layout so a scan pays for none of it.

**THE ACCOUNT IS DRAWN FROM THE READ THE PAGE ALREADY MAKES, AND THE LAYOUT MAKES NONE.** `getActiveUser()` is kept once per render with React's `cache`, so the page's gate makes the one read, inside its own ops scope, and `takePageUser()` hands the layout that read without starting one. **One turn of waiting is what makes that true, and the order behind it was measured rather than assumed.** Next starts a layout's Server Component before its page's — the page is a sibling later in the same render — and every tools page calls its gate first, so by the next turn the page has started the read. Logged on `next dev` and on a production build served by `next start` on an empty port, on all five tools routes as they then stood: the page started the read 0 to 6 ms after the layout began, under the page's own label, and the layout found it every time. The counts did not move — `/tools` 5, a tool's page 4, `/t/` 0, a tool item's 6, the label screen 3 — on both builds, and again on a production build after #457 took the label screen into a dialog. **The layout making the read with the page sharing it was the alternative, and #460 measured why not**: a layout's read lands outside every page's scope, so the page reads one fewer and the read is nobody's. **If a later Next renders the page later than one turn, the account is not drawn** — nothing read, nothing hung — and `lib/authz.js:takePageUser` says so where the next reader of it will look. **What a new tools page owes the account is its gate before any await that has to wait**: rendered through Next 16.2.10's own Flight server with `getActiveUser` and `takePageUser` as `lib/authz.js` has them, awaiting `params` — which a request render is handed already settled — or two hundred settled promises ahead of `requireUser()` left the account drawn, while a stand-in for an Airtable read, a file read, a timer or `setImmediate` there each left it undrawn, with no error.

**THE LAYOUT WAITS, AND A PROMISE WAS TRIED FIRST.** Handed to the rail as a promise and taken under Suspense, the account arrived with the page on the list and in a late segment of its own on the other three screens, put in place by an inline script — and in the hidden browser pane, which draws no frames, never placed. Awaited in the layout, it is in the HTML with everything else, and the wait costs nothing: the page waits on the same read, and with no `loading.js` nothing is sent before the page is done.

**A LAYOUT NEVER GATES.** A layout renders once and is kept across the navigations under it, so a gate there would stand in front of the first page and none after; the gate is each page's `requireUser()`, as before. `offline/authz-structure.mjs` fails a layout calling a gate or a session reader.

**SIGNED OUT, NOTHING IS READ.** No session is `getCurrentUser()` answering null before any Airtable call, so the page's gate resolves to nobody, the account is not drawn, and the page sends the reader to `/login`. Measured on `materials.localhost`, whose cookies are its own: signed out, `/t/` 0, the tool item 0 and `/login` 0, with the scan's destination carried — `/login?destination=%2Ftool-items%2FHYE-TL-260909-001`.

**`/t/` LEFT THE LAYOUT AT THE ADDRESS IT ALWAYS HAD.** `app/(tools)/t/[labelCode]/page.js` is `app/t/[labelCode]/page.js`: a route group is a directory and not a segment, so no address moved and a label already printed reaches the same page. `offline/tool-routes.mjs` pins the template by value, finds the one page answering it and holds it outside the group with no layout but the root's above it; `TOOLS_ROUTES` is the screens and `LABEL_ROUTE` the printed address. The gate stays where it was, on the tool item's page.

**WHAT IT SAYS IS THE DESIGN'S, IN THE BASE'S WORDS.** The name is the reader's own address less its domain (`lib/userName.js:selfName`): a reader naming themselves needs the unambiguous answer, which the root screen gives as the whole address, and the design draws `lee`. The avatar is its first letter as the address spells it. The role is every one the reader holds — `President`, `Admin` for the office, both when one person is both, as this base's `soo@` is — and `Employee` only when it is the whole. The button's name and its collapsed tooltip are one string, `soo, President, Admin`: the design's tooltip parted the two with a middle dot where its name has a comma, and a screen reader may say the dot. **`account` names two things on this axis**, the rail's cell here and a registration's account of what it wrote (#449), and the identifiers keep them apart — `RailAccount` and `accountOf` against `readRegistrationAccount` and `accountToTell`.

**THE MENU IS WAI-ARIA'S MENU BUTTON, ON 0a's MENU.** `ActionMenu` in `app/components/Menu.js` is 0a Menu's other half: 140 to 280 by its longest item, items 32, 5 inside and 6 from its opener — above it, since the account sits at the rail's foot. It takes DOM focus, which the field list never does, and its keys are `lib/controls.js`'s. Its one item submits the root screen's own `POST /api/auth/logout`.

**MEASURED IN A BROWSER, with the live session at 1440, 1281, 1279 and 375.** Collapsed, a 32 round button 8 above the foot, the avatar centered at (28, 864) on Face with `s` in Accent; expanded, a 224 × 48 row at the Group corner with the avatar on the same point, the name and the role 1 apart, and the chevron 13 in Ink 3. At 375 there is none. Tab goes from the last section to the account, whose tooltip is its name, 10 beside it; Down and Up open the menu on its item, Escape closes it and hands focus back, Tab closes it and goes on to the page; a press opens it, a second closes it, a press outside closes it. In a Panel at 1279 the first Escape closes the menu and the second the Panel, and a press outside closes both. **Sign out was pressed on `materials.localhost` only**: once on a session already there, and once after a sign-in by code as `soo+code471@` — one `Auth Tokens` row created and the same row spent, `Users` read only — which landed back on the scanned tool item, drew `soo+code471, Employee`, and read 6 there as `soo@`'s session does. Under `next dev` the sign-out's redirect names `localhost` (`authorization.md`), and back on `materials.localhost` every tools address went to `/login`.

**WHAT THE CHECKS HOLD.** `offline/tool-routes.mjs` holds the printed address as above. `offline/navigation.mjs` holds what `accountOf` says for each role a reader can hold, that the button's name and its tooltip are one value read off `RailAccount`'s AST, that only the rail draws the account, that the layout calls `takePageUser` once and no gate, and that `Sign out` posts where the root screen's does. `offline/dialog-frame.mjs` holds the menu's keys and the roles and names the pattern requires of the menu and its opener; `offline/authz-structure.mjs` fails a layout calling a gate or a session reader; `offline/user-name.mjs` holds `selfName`; and `offline/design-values.mjs` holds the seven names declared here and the three read before #463, with 111 names read and 77 waiting. **Sixty-one mutations were run and all sixty-one fail the tier** — fourteen against what the account says, sixteen against the menu's keys, seven against how the menu and its opener are wired, four against the layout and the rail, five against the printed address, nine against the values, one moving `Sign out`, and five blinding the checks' own readers — and every assertion this issue added fails under at least one.

## The tools screens' look (#463)

The tool item page is drawn the design's way: 0n's record page at a desk (1c, 1d) and 1f's app screen on a phone, with 1g's three states and 1l's. In `app/(tools)/tool-items/[toolItemId]/`, `ToolItemTransition.js` holds the transition's one answer and the retirement's question, `TransitionDialog.js` is the desk's press and dialog, `TransitionBar.js` the phone's foot bar, `TransitionRefusal.js` where a refused press is said, and `MoreActions.js` the menu that holds the retirement; `RetirementDialog.js` is gone, and `app/(tools)/StatusMark.js` draws the three statuses' marks. In `app/components/`, `TopBar.js` is the phone's head, `BottomBar.js` the foot bar the sign-in steps drew first, `Tooltip.js` the rail's tooltip with a second placement, `Space.js` a space the text holds at a design width, and `Notice` the alert the sign-in steps drew; `SheetField` took an icon, `SheetChip` is the foot bar's job pill, `ActionMenu` took the record page's look, and `Instant.js` draws the design's date. The two lists are drawn in `ListFrame.js` from `ListTable.js`'s parts, with `Dot.js`, `scrollLane.js` and `Controls.js`'s `Checkbox`; `offline/tool-list-view.mjs` holds them. `offline/tool-item-view.mjs`, `offline/tool-transition.mjs`, `offline/dialog-frame.mjs`, `offline/design-values.mjs`, `offline/instant-rendering.mjs`, `offline/user-name.mjs` and `offline/tool-status.mjs` hold them.

**THE DESIGN'S CASE REACHED THE BASE, PER AXIS.** A screen prints a select value as stored, so `In stock`, `Checked out` and `Checked in` — the design's sentence case on every tools screen — are the options' names now, renamed in place in the Airtable UI as #455 renamed `Registered`: a cell holds its choice by id, so every row reads the new word with no row written. The materials axis keeps the base's title case until the design settles its screens, and `naming.md` holds the rule. Writes carry no `typecast`, so every place that spells an option — the constants, the creation script, the checks, the field descriptions and these notes — changed in the commit that reads the new names. **The records above spell the options as the base held them when each was written**, `In Stock` and `Checked Out` among them; they are what was true then, and the rename carried every row they count.

**ONE ANSWER, TWO DRAWINGS.** A desk asks the transition in the header — the press, or the dialog #458 built — and a phone asks it in a foot bar whose fields stay on the page (1f), and the design says a refused press in the title block (1g).
