So the action's answer is held once, in `ToolItemTransition`, which the page renders under: two `useActionState`s would be two answers to one press the first time a window crossed the phone's edge between the press and the answer. The open rule the dialog had moved with it, since the header's sentence and the dialog's actions are the two places a refusal can stand. A landing still remounts the tree under the caller, so every opening, choice and answer goes with it; a refusal re-renders in place (#378) and keeps the answer.

**THE HEADING IS THE TOOL'S NAME, AND THE HISTORY READS NEWEST FIRST.** Both are the design's on both widths (1c, 1f). The id stays the first thing either width shows beside the name — under it at a desk with the job, in the phone's top bar — so the confirmation against the sticker is where it was (#411). The newest entry first is the order the rows came in turned over, `newestFirst`, since the link array is creation order and the log is append-only. The tab keeps the id: the design draws no tab, and the id is what tells two tabs of one tool apart.

**A STALE PRESS NAMES WHO RECORDED FIRST, AND THAT COSTS TWO READS ON THAT PATH ALONE.** 1g's sentence is `Jisoo Park checked this out a moment ago. Your check-out wasn't saved.`, so `refuseStale` reads the tool item's latest `Tool Log` row — the link array's last, through `findChildRecords` as one id — and the person who recorded it. The person is named in full (`actorName`), the moment is the row's own `Event At` in the app's notation where the design says `a moment ago` — `on 09/25/2026 2:14 PM`, the way `SEND_COPY.sent` says when an order went out — and the reader's zone writes it. The noun takes its hyphen, `check-out`, which the files of 2026-10-05 write too, where those before wrote `checkout`; the verb takes none. A read that fails says `readSubmission`'s sentence, which names nobody, rather than turning the refusal into an error.

**THE FOOT BAR'S PRESS ALWAYS ACTS, AND SAYS WHAT IS MISSING (1g).** A desk's dialog holds its commitment back until the job and the name are given (0f); the bar keeps its press, and a press with either missing is answered under the field it is about, both at once when both are, by `fieldRefusals` — the reading `readSubmission` makes field by field, so the bar and the action cannot disagree. A job not chosen is `Choose a job.`, the registration's word for a field at its placeholder, where `readSubmission` said `Choose a job you are assigned to.` for any job it did not hold. The bar draws no move notice, which the design draws on the desk's dialog alone, and its job takes `chosenJobId` — #469's re-start, moved out of the dialog when the bar became its second reader.

**A RETIRED TOOL SHOWS NOTHING IN THE ACTIONS' PLACE AT A DESK, AND THE PHONE SAYS IT IN THE FOOT BAR.** 1c draws a retired tool with no actions and no sentence, and 1f-c's foot bar holds `Nothing more can be recorded here.` — the design's terminal sentence, which names no status where #363's was built from it. A reader on no job still reads why where the controls would be, at both widths.

**THE RETIREMENT IS A MENU ITEM BEHIND `More actions`.** 1c puts it behind a 32 icon button beside the transition, 1f behind the top bar's 48; both are WAI-ARIA's menu button, as the account at the rail's foot is, and both are named `More actions`, the desk's tooltip the same string — the design names the phone's `More`, and a name nobody sees follows this app's rule. Choosing the item hands focus to its button before the question opens, so every way out of the question comes back to the button. The menu is `ActionMenu`'s record look: set against its opener's right end, at a desk the dots' ink meeting the content's edge and items at 13, on a phone 0a's Menu — 232 wide, 12 from the edge, 4 under the top bar, rows 48 at 17 in red.

**THE DATE IS THE DESIGN'S ON EVERY SCREEN.** Every date the Tools and Invoices files draw reads `09/14/2026` with its slashes dimmed, the time after it with no comma, so `INSTANT_FORMAT` and `DAY_FORMAT` take two-figure months and days and `Instant.js` draws the parts `instantParts` hands it — the materials screens' instants with the tools axis's, which is why the date's four names are the one exception `offline/design-values.mjs` allows a file every screen reaches. The locale is `en-US` for every reader: the zone stays the reader's (#374), and a browser set to another language wrote the date in that language's order, the one part of an English screen that was not. A sentence takes the same figures with plain slashes (`instantText`); the order document takes the two-figure month and day under its own zone, since it spreads the same options. A calendar date — an invoice's due date, a delivery's received date — is a stored string and not an instant, and keeps its form until its screen is drawn.

**THE ROOM BETWEEN TWO VALUES IS A SPACE IN THE TEXT AS WELL.** The 9 between a date and its time and the 9 either side of a dot were padding first, and padding is room and not a character: the text read `10/05/20268:55 AM` and `HYE-TL-260909-00726-DEMO-01`, a copy and an assistive reader both, which no screen had done while a time was one string. So each is a `Space`, a real space in a box of the design's width that keeps it from collapsing, and the box rather than the font's own space sets the figure. Measured after the change: 9.0 from a date to its time at a desk and 8.0 on a phone, 9.0 either side of a dot, and an entry's line copies as `10/05/2026 8:55 AM · 26-DEMO-01 · by Soo Choi`. #457's dialog drew its count and its size apart the padded way, which was not this issue's to change, and draws `Dot.js` since #495.

**WHO DID SOMETHING IS NAMED IN FULL.** The history's `by` and the stale press's sentence name a person as the one who recorded, and the design draws them as first and last name, so `actorName` prints both — and the address's local part for a row missing either, the fallback every rendering takes for a nameless row, where a picker's `fullUserName` prints a first name alone. The materials screens' places of the same kind — a request's history, `Recorded by` on a delivery and an invoice — still print the first name until their screens take the design; `naming.md` and `authorization.md` say so.

**THE LABEL IN THE RAIL IS THE DIALOG'S PAGE, ON THE DIALOG'S FACE.** 0p draws a label on screen at whatever size reads, over the Field wash, with the print size in text, and 1c draws the dialog's own page at twice its size with `Size` and `Symbol` under it; so the rail draws `LabelPreview`, the dialog's `LabelPage` at its preview scale, from the label this render built. **The files of 2026-10-01 gave 1c a 1 corner, an 8% ring and a 1 2 shadow, and 1i gave the dialog 0k's Preview** — the Mark corner, a 6% ring over 2 8 at 8% — which the spec states for a print shown on the page. #457 did not leave the first out: it drew the dialog, whose drawing matched the spec, and had no page label to draw. The rail took the dialog's face, so one label is one drawing on the page and in the dialog, the spec over a drawing as `design-system.md`'s answer 11 settled the name sheet's `Done` — **and the files of 2026-10-05 draw 1c's label on the Preview face too**, so the drawing and the spec now agree. The symbol drawn at its printed size with a sentence saying so went, as did `printedSizeNote`; a host whose symbol does not fit draws the host's sentence in the label's place. The preview is named `QR label for this tool`, which no screen had drawn since #352.

**THE FIGURES, MEASURED ON A DEV SERVER AGAINST THE LIVE BASE.** The page is **7 operations for a tool in stock and 6 for one out or retired, unchanged**: the look reads nothing, since the name, the holder and the retirement's moment come off rows the page already read, and the in-stock page's seventh is #376's names. `recordToolItemEventAction` is **7** for each of the four writes below and **3** for a refusal, both unchanged; a stale press is **5**, `refuseStale`'s two reads, and the render it triggers is the page's 6. A code no tool carries is **2**.

**WHAT A BROWSER SHOWED, AT 1440 AND AT 375.** At a desk the header's name is 24/32, the id and the job 10 under it and the status 12 under those, with the history's entries on a 20 track and the rail 336 wide; `More actions` is 32 with its dots' ink on the content's edge, its tooltip 6 above it, its menu 6 under it with its right edge on that edge, and a retired tool draws no actions and its date 14 after the status. On a phone the top bar is 56 and sticky with the id at 17, the name 22/28, and the foot bar 16 above and 20 below its rows, 12 apart: a press with no job said `Choose a job.` under a red pill and one with no name `Enter a name to check out.` under the field, the press acting both times; both sheets opened from the bar and handed focus back; a press in flight showed the spinner alone and locked the pill; the menu was 232 wide, 12 from the edge and 4 under the bar, its row 48 at 17 in Red; a retired tool's bar said `Nothing more can be recorded here.` at 16/24 in Ink 3 with no `More`; and a code no tool carries drew 1g-c's 40 mark, `Tool not found` at 22/28 and its sentence at 280. **Two tabs:** with the page open on a phone at `In stock`, a second tab checked the tool out to `Lee Park`, and the first tab's press came back with the status re-rendered to `Out` and `Lee Park` and the Notice saying `Soo Choi checked this out on 10/05/2026 8:50 AM. Your check-out wasn't saved.`, the bar offering the check-in; widened to a desk, the same sentence stood under the status in Red. **One job:** the desk's `Check in` recorded on the press, busy and not disabled with focus on it until the landing, and the phone's pill stated `26-DEMO-01` with no chevron. The materials screens' dates read the same way in their own face: `/pos/[poId]`'s `Signed at 09/11/2026 7:57 AM` and `/prs/[prId]`'s history, the slashes at 45% with 1.5 either side and the time 9 past. No history is not reachable on this base, where every tool item has its `Created` row, and was not seen.

**THE BASE GAINED FOUR `Tool Log` ROWS UNDER `HYE-TL-260909-007`, KEPT**: `-002` to `-005` — `Checked out` to `Dana K`, `Checked in`, `Checked out` to `Lee Park` and `Checked in`, all on 26-DEMO-01 — with the four cache writes after them, the tool item back at `In stock`. They prove the rename too: a write carries no `typecast`, so a row holding `Checked out` is the option taking its new name. `soo@`'s `Assigned Jobs` was narrowed to 26-DEMO-01 for the one-job walk and put back as 26-DEMO-01, 26-DEMO-02, read back in that order. The three field descriptions the rename made false were PATCHed from the creation script's spec, and a read-back found every table and field description of the three tables matching it and both selects' choices as they were.

**THE TWO LISTS HOLD THEIR HEAD AND SCROLL THEIR ROWS (1a, 1b).** `app/components/ListFrame.js` is the frame: the breadcrumb and the list's head stand outside the scroll, the rows scroll in a lane of their own with 0i's 8 reserved (`scrollLane.js`, the rail's column's bar, read now from one place) and stop at their end rather than scrolling the window (0i Chain), and the column head holds at the top of the rows — 36 on the Sticky ground, a Band under it that it owns with -1 and one over it inside the 36 (0b, 0k). Everything above and below the lane gives its 8 back, so the head, the rows and the pager share one right edge. **The column steps aside for a list**: `Rail.js`'s column scrolls every other screen, the record page's one scroll among them (0i), and reads the frame's `data-list-frame` mark with `:has()` to scroll nothing and reserve no lane when it holds one — the alternative, two lanes, put a blank 8 at the window's edge. `app/components/ListTable.js` holds what a row and a head ARE, as class strings a Server Component can import, which a client module's exports are not.

**THE PAGER IS THE ENDING, AND ITS GROUND SAYS WHETHER ROWS RUN BENEATH IT.** It is pinned at the foot over the rows, so they end on its own height and no 40 (0i End), and on the selection bar's height and 12 as well while the bar stands (0b) — 68, and 134 with the bar, the drawings' figures. Its ground is the Sticky one, white at 0.82 over the blur under a Band, while rows run beneath it, and plain white with no rule once the list has ended above it (0k, 0e): a fact about the scroll position, measured on every scroll and on any change to the lane's or the rows' size.

**THE TOOL LIST PAGES AT 25 (#463).** 1a draws the pager under the tool list too, and 0b's page of rows is 25, so `/tools?page=` is a slice of rows the page has already built — `pageOfTools`, by the clamp `pageOfToolItems` uses, one window function behind both — and costs nothing: every tool's counts need every tool item whichever page is drawn. `toolsPath` writes it, absent on the first page, and `TOOL_PAGE_SIZE` sizes both lists, its doc saying so.

**NOTHING SELECTED DRAWS NO BAR, AND THE BAR SAYS THE REST (0b).** The sentence and the print control drawn and not acting that stood over the list since #443 went: the selection bar floats 12 above the pager while any row is selected — the 32 clear, the count, `· N not on this page` while other pages hold part of it, and `Print labels` — rising 8 and fading in over 160ms, each clause holding its three-digit width, and inert while it does not show. `Escape` clears the selection unless a dialog is open, which `Escape` closes first. A selection past what one print takes keeps the bar's count and disables the control with why before it — `One print takes at most 100.` then, a state 0b did not draw, and 0b's own `Up to 100 labels per print.` since #495 (below). The page box sits in the column head with no words and is named `Select this page`, Design's (`design-system.md`, answer 3); an entry's box is named for its id.

**A TOOL'S OWN LIST READS NEWEST FIRST.** It is its `Tool Items` array turned over, as 1b draws it — `pageOfToolItems` through `newestFirst`, the turn a tool item's history takes — and a registration lands on the first page, where what it wrote begins, with every one selected; `pageHolding`, which found the page holding the first tool item written while the list read oldest first, went with that arithmetic. What the order costs was weighed and accepted: a registration pushes every row along, so a link to a later page names other tool items after one. The selection is printed ids, so it moves with nothing, and it was left as it was: a press still leaves it in ascending id, the order a run prints in, which is the list's no longer. **Seen at 1440**: a 40-item tool's page 1 opened on `HYE-TL-260930-013` and page 2 closed on `-260909-004`, its first, at 4 operations each. **A registration was not run**, since it writes to the base: its landing was opened as the action builds it, page 1 with the ids in minted order — the newest thirteen arrived checked at the top under `13 selected`, and the newest thirty filled page 1 under `30 selected · 5 not on this page`, the step to page 2 showing the other five checked at its head.

**THE TOOL LIST STAYS BY NAME, AS 1a DRAWS IT.** The files of 2026-10-02 and of 2026-10-05 both draw its rows in name order, and only 1b is marked newest first. Newest first was weighed for it too — by the row's `createdTime`, which Airtable returns with every record and no field of `Tools` holds, so it would have added no read — and dropped for the drawing's order; the other candidate, the newest tool item under each tool, would have lifted a tool to the top at each registration. The registration's suggestions keep the order the list hands them, so they read by name as well. **Seen at 1440**: the list read `DEMO Angle Grinder`, `DEMO Cordless Drill`, `DEMO Impact Driver`, `DEMO Rotary Hammer` at 5 operations, and `demo` typed into the dialog suggested the four in that order.

**WHERE THE TWO DRAWINGS PART, THE SPEC DECIDES.** 1b sets a list's count line 10 under the title and 20 under the line and its control level with the title; 1a sets the line 6 under in a 26 box and 18 under, its figure at Ink 3 and its control centered on the pair. One head draws both on 0n's figures — the Header stack's 10, every figure in Ink (0e), a two-line list header centering its one control — and the 20 under it, with 1a's 40 as the least room before the control. A row's face under the pointer and while selected is square in both drawings; 0b gives the hover on 1080 a Control radius, which both take. Each name says which drawing it came from.

**THE BOX IS THE BROWSER'S CHECKBOX UNDER THE DRAWING.** `Controls.js`'s `Checkbox` draws 0b's 16 at 0j's Badge radius on Ink 5's edge, the Accent with a white check or a dash, in a 32 target that gives its room back, over a native input — the design's is a button with a checkbox's role, and the native one already says checked, unchecked and mixed and toggles on Space. It stands above the row's link, which covers the row so the whole row opens the tool item.

**THE FIGURES, MEASURED THE SAME WAY.** `/tools` is **5 operations**, #339's four with the tool items read in two batches now that the base holds 70; `/tools/[toolRecordId]` is **4** on either page of a 40-item tool and with any selection; a record id no tool carries is **2**; and `readToolItemLabelsAction` from the selection bar is **2**. Paging and the selection read nothing.

**WHAT A BROWSER SHOWED, AT 1440.** Both lists draw their head 102 tall — 1b under its 48 breadcrumb bar — the column head 37 with the first row 1 under its top edge, rows 41, and the text 12 in from the 1080's edge, where the next step's chevron ink ends 0.5 from the counts' right edge. The column held a list without scrolling or a lane of its own, and the window never scrolled. On a 40-item tool the pager read `1–25 of 40` and `Page 1 of 2` on the Sticky ground under a Band, and plain white with no rule at the end of the rows, where the last row's foot met the pager's top; the lane's scroll events were dispatched by hand, since the hidden pane sends none. Two boxes brought the bar 12 above the pager, 262.5 wide as 1b draws it, the head's box mixed and the rows on Face, and the end room grew from 68 to 134; on page 2 the bar read `2 selected · 2 not on this page` and the next step drew without acting; `Escape` cleared it and took the bar and the room away. `Print labels` opened the labels' dialog on the selection, `Escape` closed it and kept the selection, and focus came back to the control. A record id no tool carries drew `Tool not found` at 16/24 and `Back to Tools` 14 under it. Neither empty list was seen: every tool on this base has items, and a tool without one is a registration that failed between its two writes.

**THE RECORD RAIL HOLDS UNDER THE BREADCRUMB BAR WHILE IT FITS (0i).** 0i makes a record page one scroll in which the bar holds, the header scrolls away and the rail holds under the bar while it fits; 1c's history is too short to show the last, and the drawing's rail scrolls. So the label block is sticky 24 under the bar — the 24 it stands under the header's Band — inside an aside whose Rule still runs the whole column. Measured at 1440 × 520 on `HYE-TL-261001-022`: the block's heading stood at 72 from 0 to the column's end at 400.

**THE FILES OF 2026-10-05 MOVED FOUR THINGS ON THE TOOL ITEM PAGE, AND THIS ISSUE TOOK THEM.** The desk history's dot is 6, 7 from its entry's top, where it was 5 at 8; 1c's label is on the Preview face (above); the foot bar's pill sets its icon 8 before its value and takes the Wash alone under a press, the two figures #463 had already taken from the spec over the drawing; and 1g writes `check-out` with its hyphen. What they moved on the screens other issues drew is `design-system.md`'s list, for the issue that takes it.

The screens merged before Design's last sending take what it settled, and `design-system.md` lists each against what the app draws: 0f's Working, 0l's message about a whole dialog, 0i's thumb and its chain, the account's menu at a desk and on a phone, the list a field opens, the labels' dialog's two panes and its dot, 1j's line under an empty name, 1k's fork, 0b past a hundred and 1g's sentence.

**A BUSY ACTION KEEPS ITS RESTING WIDTH, AND THE SPINNER IS ALL IT SHOWS.** #469's one cell held a busy action at its working label's width as well as its resting one's, which stood `Check out` at 154.69 where 1c draws it as wide as its word. The final files keep the resting width and draw the spinner alone in it, so `Button` lays what it shows while busy over its label, and the `-ing` word stays for assistive tech; an action that spans its column — a sign-in step's — keeps the spinner and the word from the phone's edge up (0f). **The labels' two openers are busy while their page reads the labels**: the dialog opens only once `readToolItemLabelsAction` answers, and until #495 nothing said the press had been taken. Their word is `Loading…`, since the press reads the labels rather than printing them — 0f's rule that the working word names the work being done.

**A MESSAGE ABOUT A WHOLE DIALOG IS INK 2, AND ITS ROLE IS STILL `alert`.** 0l draws it behind the 16 info mark in Ink 2, with red for a field alone, and `Controls.js`'s `Refusal` draws it so — a dialog's line above its actions, and the tool item page's under its status at a desk. The final files mark such a line `status`. This app gives every refusal of the reader's own press `alert` — a field's, a dialog's, the Notice and the tool item page's — since the sentence answers what the reader just did, and an `alert` inserted with its text is announced where a `status` arriving with its content may not be. The ink changed and the role did not.

**THE NAME LIST'S HEAD STANDS ONLY OVER THE RECENT NAMES.** `Recently at this job` names what the list holds while nothing is typed; once a fragment is, the list is the names matching it, so the head goes and each name shows the part that matched at 600. `namesAreRecent` and `matchedPart` in `lib/toolTransition.js` say both, the match found where the narrowing finds it — on the same fold, and at a word's start before an earlier place inside a word — and the desk's list and the phone's name sheet read the two.

The notice's `All 5 were added and work as usual.` is not taken: a landing with no shortfall carries no count. ## The column head over its rows, the hand, and a menu's focus (#501)

Three things Soo saw on the deployed tools screens, and the rail's head beside them. `app/components/ListTable.js` holds what stands over what in a list, `app/globals.css` the hand (`design-system.md` has the reasoning and what it changed above the axes), `app/components/Menu.js` an item's focus, and `app/components/Rail.js` the room under its head.

**A ROW IS ITS OWN STACKING CONTEXT, SO ITS BOX STAYS UNDER THE COLUMN HEAD.** The head is sticky at `z-10`, and a row's box sat at `z-10` too, lifted over the link that covers the row (#463); a row made no context of its own, so the box and the head shared one, and the box — later in the document — drew over the head as its row scrolled under it: read at 1440 × 900 on a tool's page scrolled 120, the top element inside the head's band was `HYE-TL-260930-012`'s box. A row is `isolate` now, so what it lifts rises inside it. The order is the rows, the column head, the pager at the head's `z-10` after the lane with the selection bar inside it, the rail's Panel at `z-20`, and the top layer — 1b draws 1, 3, 3, 4 and 35. Lowering the box alone was weighed and left: the next thing a row lifted would rise over the head again. The tool list draws no box, so its head held already, and it takes the same row.

**AN ITEM SHOWS FOCUS ONLY WHEN THE KEYBOARD PUT IT THERE.** `ActionMenu` moves focus onto an item as it opens, and its plain item drew `:focus` the way it draws the pointer, so a press on the account met `Sign out` shaded. It draws `focus-visible` now, as the destructive item had since #463, so `More actions` was right already. The field list's shaded option is the one `aria-activedescendant` names — the chosen one as a choice opens, as a native select shows it — and stays, since it is a selection and not focus. **One case the browser decides otherwise**: a press on an opener that already holds focus the keyboard gave it, as after `Escape`, moves no focus, so the browser keeps the keyboard's modality and the item opens shown.

**THE RAIL'S HEAD AND ITS SECTIONS PART BY 16 OF ROOM**, as the files of 2026-10-01's second sending and every one after draw the rail collapsed and expanded alike, where a rule stood with 10 either side (`design-system.md` has how the comparisons missed it). A Panel is the same rail laid over the column, so it takes the same room; the sections stand at 60 from the rail's top, where they stood at 65.

**WHAT A BROWSER SHOWED**, on the dev server with the session already there, the pane's own events dispatched and nothing written. At 1440 × 900 on a 41-item tool's page scrolled 100 by a wheel, the head's own content was on top where the boxes of `HYE-TL-260930-013` and `-012` passed under it, the selection bar and the pager on top of the rows under them, and a press on a box selected it rather than opening the row; `/tools` at 1440 × 300, scrolled to its end, held its head over both rows under it. Under the pointer every control that acts read `pointer` — the rail's toggle, a section, the account, the breadcrumb, `Add tools`, both boxes, a row, the pager's next step, the bar's clear and `Print labels`, a tool item's `Check out`, `Print label` and `More actions`, a dialog's close, `Cancel` and its job, and on a sign-in step `Continue`, the chip's `Change` and `Resend email` — while the pager's step at its end, `Print labels` held busy, the dialog's `Check out` held back and the chip while sending read `default`, and `Resend in`, `Email sent` and the sending line read as text. A press opened the account's menu and `More actions` with the item focused and nothing shaded, `Enter` opened them with it shown — Hover, and red's Face in red — and `Escape` handed focus back. Collapsed and expanded at 1440 and as a Panel at 1279 the head stood 12 to 44 and the sections from 60, with no line between, and the Panel covered the list's head, its pager and a row. **The figures are unchanged**: no read was added.

**WHAT THE CHECKS HOLD, AND WHAT A MUTATION SHOWED.** `offline/tool-list-view.mjs` holds the order — a row `isolate`, the box over a cover that lifts nothing, the head over the rows, the pager at its layer after the lane, the Panel over both — beside a planted list doing each wrong; `offline/dialog-frame.mjs` that no look an item takes draws on a bare `:focus` and every one draws `focus-visible`, and the hand's one base-layer rule with both of its exclusions and the states that set them; `offline/navigation.mjs` the rail's head with no rule and its room; and `offline/design-values.mjs` the new name by value. **32 mutations were run**, one at a time in a copy of the tree with abfa156 as the base for what is new, and each of the 22 labels this issue added failed under at least one, with none surviving.

## Three tables, and what each is for

`Tools` is the KIND. `Tool Items` is the object. `Tool Log` is the history.

**A tool is bought BY THE KIND and tracked ONE AT A TIME.** A company buys six of the same drill and then needs six labels, six locations and six histories under one name that a person typed once. Folding the kind and the object into one table gives you either six rows repeating the name — which is how two spellings of one kind appear and a count splits in two — or one row that cannot say where any individual drill is.

**`Tools` takes no minted ID, the way `Vendors` and `Materials` take none.** Nothing prints a kind and nobody quotes one, so the name a person types is the identity. The cost is a uniqueness rule the schema cannot hold, since Airtable has no unique constraint: `upsertTool` enforces it, find-or-create under a lock, and `getToolByName` is the comparison it makes — `Impact Driver` and `impact driver` are one tool whose count must not split.

**THIS PARAGRAPH SAID THAT LOOKUP WAS CASE-INSENSITIVE BECAUSE AIRTABLE'S `=` IS, AND AIRTABLE'S `=` IS NOT (#338).** Measured read-only against this base before anything was written: `{Vendor Name} = "brazos metals"` returned 0 rows against a stored `Brazos Metals`, the uppercased form returned 0, and `LOWER(TRIM({Vendor Name})) = LOWER(TRIM(…))` returned 1 for both. So the gate as #334 wrote it would have created a second tool under a differently cased name — the exact split this paragraph says it prevents, in the one table where the name IS the identity. The claim was in `lib/airtable/tools.js`'s header too and both are corrected. **Neither form collapses an internal whitespace run** (`Brazos  Metals` matched 0 rows either way), which is why the write side normalizes: `LOWER(TRIM(…))` is enough only because `normalizeItemText` has already collapsed the runs on both the stored value and the argument, which is exactly what `itemNaming.js` says about `Materials`.

**`Tool Items.Tool Item ID` is different in kind from every other minted ID on this base**, and it is worth saying why once. A PR ID is read on a screen; a PO ID is read on a document. This one is encoded into a QR code, glued to a drill and carried onto a site. Two rows sharing it means two tools wearing the same label, and the repair is reprinting both — see the ID section below for the width and how a batch stays contiguous.

**`Tool Log` exists because a status field answers where a tool is now and nothing about the project that just ended.** The question a site asks when a job closes is which tools went out on it — a question about the past, which the current status cannot answer once the tool has moved on. It is append-only in the shape `PR Edit Log` already has: a row records what was true at a moment, a moment does not change, so there is no update function and correcting a mistaken scan is another row.

### The name is #333's rule applied, not a coincidence

`Tools` → `Tool Items` is a child table taking its parent's name. `Tool Log` is an append-only event table taking its subject and `Log`, which is the second half of the same rule and is why it is not `Tool Item Log`: `PR Edit Log`'s subject is a PR edit rather than a `Purchase Requests` row spelled out, and the pattern is a short form of the subject plus `Log`.

`Tool Items` is also a fifth item table under #303's rule, so a row of it is a **tool item** — in the code, the schema and these notes. **On screen it is the design's since #455**: a `tool` in a sentence and an `item` on its tool's own page. This said never a bare `item`, because on this base the word already names a row of `PR Items`, `PO Items`, `Invoice Items` or `Delivery Items` — stricter than #303 itself, which drops the modifier where no second kind of item row is on the string, and no tools screen shows one.

**AND A `Tools` ROW IS A `tool`, WHICH #338 SETTLED BECAUSE IT WAS ABOUT TO BE SETTLED BY ACCIDENT.** The same rule decides it — a row takes its own table's name in the singular — and nothing else may borrow the word, so a tool item is never "a tool". What made this worth writing down is that `kind` had begun to look like the answer: this file, `getAllTools`'s docstring and #336's own brief all say "kind", which is the right EXPLANATORY word for what distinguishes the two tables and the wrong one for a screen. A brief handed to the design work saying "a list of tool kinds" invites `Kind` as a column head, and that brief is corrected in #338's commit. `kind` stays in prose here, where it is explaining the split rather than naming a row. **#455 IS WHERE THE SCREEN STOPPED FOLLOWING THIS**: the design calls a tool item a `tool` in every sentence about one, which borrows the `Tools` table's word, and `naming.md` records it as a divergence with the design as its owner — kept honest by restructuring the few sentences that meet the kind and its units at once, so one word never names two rows in one sentence. The code and these notes keep the pair.

## Status is written by this app, not computed by Airtable

`Tool Items."Status"` is a cache of the last `Tool Log` row, written by `lib/toolStatus.js`'s mapping in the same operation as the row that moved it. That is a decision rather than a limitation worked around, and **#335 removed one of the three grounds it rested on — the conclusion survives on the other two.** The removal is recorded rather than quietly dropped, so nobody re-derives the argument from a premise that is gone.

**THE GROUND THAT DIED.** #334's first and strongest reason was that a last row is not a status: the vocabularies were different sizes and `Job Changed` was a last row that left the status alone, so reading the last row's `Event` into `Status` would have written a value the option list did not contain. #335 removed that event, `STATUS_AFTER_EVENT` has no `null` entry any more, and every event now moves the status. A formula that copied the latest event's status would, on today's vocabulary, be correct.

**Airtable has no argmax.** A rollup's `MAX` is numeric, and there is no aggregation that returns the value of a field on the row where another field is highest. "The status implied by the row with the latest `Event At`" is not expressible. The workaround is to lean on rollup ordering — the link cell's order — which is not a documented guarantee, so a history that reordered for any reason would silently rewrite every tool's status.

**A formula field cannot be a `singleSelect`.** The closed option list goes, the colors go, and #339's count per status would be computed over free text. `Tool Items."Job"` has the same problem one step worse: a formula cannot be a link either, so the job cache could not exist at all on that route.

Neither of the two is checkable in this repository — both are facts about Airtable — which is why this paragraph is prose and `offline/tool-status.mjs` says so in the assertion that replaced #334's.

## Why the vocabulary is four events and three statuses

**Statuses: `In stock`, `Out`, `Retired`. Events: `Created`, `Checked out`, `Checked in`, `Retired`** — sentence case since #463, and `The words, sentence-cased` below is where the case came from. #334 shipped five and eight; #335 cut them to this before a single row existed, which is the only reason it was affordable — an existing select's option list cannot be PATCHed at all, so the repair was deleting two tables and running the creation script again.

**`In Repair`, `Lost` and `Found` are gone because those things do not happen here.** A tool that breaks is thrown away and replaced rather than repaired, and nobody reports an individual tool item missing. The cost of modelling them anyway is not neutral: a status nobody will ever designate is a permanent blank in the per-status count, a dead option in the list filter, and an entry in `STATUS_AFTER_EVENT` that no code path reaches. If repair ever starts, adding an option is a hand edit in the Airtable UI — known, and cheaper than carrying three dead values until then.

**`Retired` stays because without a terminal status the count goes wrong.** A discarded tool would sit `In stock` for good, and the only other way to correct that is deleting the record, which takes its whole log with it. It covers a tool found missing at a stock check as well as one thrown away, because on this axis those are one fact — the company no longer holds it.

**AND NOTHING RECORDS WHICH OF THE TWO IT WAS, WHICH IS A DECISION AND NOT AN OMISSION (#363).** This paragraph used to end "and which of the two it was goes in `Tool Log."Notes"`", and that field is deleted. The question to ask of the distinction is what would read it, and the answer is nothing: every question this axis serves — where is this tool, what went out on that job, how many of this tool does the company still hold — is answered by the status and the log's jobs, and none of them changes if the tool was skipped or stolen. What a stored reason would have bought is a report nobody has asked for, against a required field on the one transition that has to be quick enough to actually get used. **So if somebody later asks why there is no `Lost` status and no lost-versus-discarded reason, the answer is that both were weighed — `Lost` in #335, the reason in #363 — and both were found to have no reader.** What would reopen it is a real question that needs the difference, and the repair is cheap: `Retired` stays one status and a `Lost` one is a hand edit to an option list, exactly as this file already records for `In Repair`.

**`Job Changed` is gone for a different reason: the fact it recorded is already in the log.** A manager scans out their own job's tools to workers; a worker may carry one to another site; whoever manages the site it reaches scans it back in. So a tool moving between jobs shows up as a check-out on one job and the next check-in on another, and a separate event adds nothing those two rows do not already carry. **That is what makes `Tool Items."Job"` a cache rather than an attribute** — see below.

**`Created` — `Registered` until #455 — was added, and the deciding reason is a field that is not there.** #334 left `Created At` off `Tool Items` on the ground that the log's first row holds the instant. Without an event naming the act there is nowhere at all that answers when a tool item came into existence, and `Created At` would have to come back. `Checked in` was the alternative and reads wrong: it means a tool came back into stock, and one just created has never been out. It is also not a fiction — the label still has to be printed and stuck on, so the `In stock` it leaves behind describes a real tool sitting on a bench.

**The naming shapes.** A transition a person designates is named for the state it leaves the tool in, so the event and the status are the same string: `Retired`, the only one of that kind left. A transition that happens by scanning carries an action name, because the person is performing an act rather than declaring a state: `Checked out`, `Checked in`. `Created` is a third case — an act with no status of its own name — which is why `STATUS_AFTER_EVENT` is a map rather than something derivable from the two lists.

**What the check can and cannot hold.** `offline/tool-status.mjs` asserts that every status is produced by at least one event, which catches a dead option. It is **not** the rule applied above: what was removed were statuses nobody would ever DESIGNATE, and `In Repair` was perfectly reachable from `Sent to Repair` right up until it was deleted. The assertion would have passed on the old vocabulary and passes on the new one. Whether an event describes something that happens on a site is not a property of this source tree, and a green run is not evidence that the vocabulary is justified.

## The job is a cache on the tool item and a fact on every log row

`Tool Log."Job"` is filled on every row and never blank. It holds **the job the tool item was on at that event** — for a scan, where it is immediately after; for the one designated event, where it was.

**`Tool Items."Job"` IS A CACHE OF THAT COLUMN ON THE LATEST ROW — the same kind of value as `Status`, from the same row.** #334's description called it the job the tool belongs to, a durable attribute, and #335 corrected that: a tool does not belong to a job, it was last scanned on one. Nothing in the flow ever reassigns a tool; it moves because somebody carries it and somebody else scans it in.

It is still REQUIRED and still app-enforced, since Airtable cannot make a link field required (the limit `Invoice Items."PO Item"` lives with, #278). Never empty, because every event carries a job and registration is an event.

### Where the job comes from

**THE INVARIANT AND THE MECHANISM ARE TWO THINGS, AND THIS SECTION FUSED THEM UNTIL #363.** It read "Registration, check-out and check-in all take it from the `Users."Assigned Jobs"` of whoever performs the action" — which is a sentence about a MECHANISM, enumerating three events because three existed. Stated as a rule it invited a fourth event to join the list, and #363 did join it, and the base showed within a day that the fourth does not belong there. What is universal is the sentence above: **the job is where the tool item was at that event.** The actor's assignment is how three events learn it.

**A SCANNED EVENT TAKES IT FROM THE ACTOR, and that is sound because the actor is holding the tool.** Registration, check-out and check-in all read `Users."Assigned Jobs"`: one assigned job is used without asking, several are chosen from, and there is no path anywhere that types a job. In all three the person and the tool are in the same place, so the actor's assignment IS the tool's location.

**THE DESIGNATED EVENT TAKES IT FROM THE TOOL ITEM, because designating is not handling.** Retiring moves nothing and the person doing it need not be near the tool, so a `Retired` row inherits `Tool Items."Job"` — where it was. `TOOL_EVENT`'s own docstring already splits the vocabulary on exactly this line, scanned against designated, so the split is not new; what #363 found is that the job follows it too.

**AND THE COST OF GETTING THAT WRONG IS ON THIS BASE RATHER THAN IN A HYPOTHETICAL.** `HYE-TL-260909-004` was checked out on `26-DEMO-01`, checked in on `26-DEMO-02`, and then retired on `26-DEMO-01` because #363's first shape asked the actor for a job a second time. The row and the cache it fed both claimed a site the tool had never been on, and `which tools were on 26-DEMO-02` — the question `toolLog.js`'s own header gives as this table's reason for existing — lost it. Worse in the case `Retired` exists for: a tool found missing at a stock check has the site that lost it overwritten by whoever noticed. What the actor's job buys on that row is nothing any question reads, and who did it is `Recorded By`.

**FROM THE CACHE RATHER THAN FROM THE PREVIOUS ROW**, which are the same value by construction. The cache is never blank even when the history is — #338's failed log pass leaves a tool item with a job and no rows — and the action already holds the record, where reading the log would cost an operation on the screen a scan lands on.

**IT IS STORED ON THE LOG ROW AT THAT MOMENT AND NEVER LOOKED UP AFTERWARDS, WHICHEVER SOURCE IT CAME FROM.** `Assigned Jobs` changes when a person moves site, so a log row that referenced the session or the user's current assignment would make an old check-out describe today's posting. That is the precise thing the per-row copy exists to prevent, and referencing it at read time would undo the whole reason `Tool Log` carries a job of its own. **Inheriting does not touch that rule**: it copies an immutable stored value at WRITE time, which is the same shape a scan's copy takes.

**THE NEVER-BLANK INVARIANT IS HELD AT THE WRITER NOW.** `createToolLogEntry` wrote `[]` for a missing job, which nothing could reach while every caller resolved one out of the actor's assignments; a source that is a nullable field changes that, so it throws instead, the way `createToolItems` already does for the same limit. `Recorded By` took the same guard in the same commit, since `logRowFacts` renders both on every row on the strength of the same claim.

### `Former Job` was considered and refused

With no blanks, the PREVIOUS row's `Job` is unambiguously the previous job — a check-in on a different job than the check-out before it IS the record of a tool changing site. Storing the pair would be one fact in two places, derivable from an ordering the log already has, and #340 renders the whole history at once so it holds both rows.

**#363's INHERITANCE IS WHAT KEEPS THAT READING HONEST FOR THE FOURTH EVENT.** A `Retired` row carrying the actor's job would differ from the row above it whenever somebody elsewhere retired the tool, and by the rule in this section that difference reads as a move — a tool changing site on its way to being discarded, which never happened. Inheriting makes a retirement always agree with the row before it, so the only rows that disagree are the ones where the tool really moved.

The counter-precedent is `Delivery Items."Former PO Item"` (#167), which IS stored — and the difference is the reason: there is no log on that axis, so the re-attachment destroys the only record of where the row came from. Here the log is the record.

## What the schema deliberately does NOT carry

- **No `Created At` on `Tool Items`.** The `Tool Item ID` carries the day and the log's first row carries the instant; a third copy is the shape this base keeps having to remove. **That omission is what forced the `Created` event** — with no such row there would be nowhere left holding the instant, and the field would have to come back. The two decisions hold each other up, so neither can be undone alone.
- **No `Created At` on `Tools`.** `Vendors` and `Materials`, the two tables it is modelled on, have none, and `/tools` orders by name. An Airtable `createdTime` can be added in one field CREATE if #339 wants a tiebreak.
- **No per-status rollup on `Tools`.** #339 needs a count per status and can have it from the `Tool Items` link array `getAllTools` already returns for free. The measurable condition for changing that: if `/tools` measures above roughly ten operations because of the tool item walk, the count moves into a rollup and `Purchase Orders."Uninvoiced Items"` (#244) is the worked example of the move. Five rollups nothing reads would be five fields to keep in step for nothing.
- **No `{Parent} Record ID` lookup on either child table.** After #334 deleted the seven that existed, `Material Prices` holds the only two left on the base, and those are CLAUDE.md's stated exception — a price row is keyed by two links and has no parent whose reverse-link would do. See below.
- **No `Unit` field anywhere.** A tool item is one object, not a quantity, so `add_unit_options.py` stays a five-table script — the same note `create_direct_purchases_272.py` makes for its own table.

## The tool item ID: width, and how a batch stays contiguous (#335)

`HYE-TL-YYMMDD-###`, minted by `lib/ids.js:generateNextToolItemIds`. Same daily-reset shape as every other document in the system, counting the rows whose ID carries the same prefix and never a date field (#164) — the rule that matters more here than anywhere else, because a duplicate is two labels already stuck to two tools and the repair is reprinting both.

**THE WIDTH IS 3 AND IT IS THE FIRST PER-FAMILY WIDTH ON THIS BASE.** Every other family takes `SEQ_PAD_LENGTH`, which is 2 and stays 2: `formatSequentialId` reads `padLength` through a default, so `ID_KINDS.TOOL_ITEM` declaring one leaves the other five untouched. That mechanism is not new — `CHILD_KINDS` already carries a width per relation — and it is the reason widening this sequence changed no document ID.

Three, because one registration creates many. Every other family is a document somebody raises one at a time and none has ever needed more than a handful in a day; a tool registration takes a quantity, and the first day of use is a warehouse's whole stock arriving at once. It is also not a new number — seven of the nine child relations already pad to 3.

**IT IS NOT A CEILING, WHICH IS WHY THE CHOICE IS CHEAP.** `padStart` does not truncate and `nextSequence` parses the whole tail, so the 1000th tool item of one day is `-1000` rather than a collision. The width is a statement about how the common case reads.

**A BATCH IS ONE LOCK, ONE QUERY AND N CONTIGUOUS IDS.** `mintDailyIds` holds the critical section across the whole registration; `mintDailyId` is that function with a count of 1. Minting one at a time would be wrong twice: a lock acquisition and a full day-prefix query PER tool item, which grows with the day, and — worse — two people registering at once would interleave, so one registration's tool items would come back with another's numbers scattered through them.

The direction of the delegation is forced rather than chosen: `offline/id-sequence.mjs` asserts `lib/ids.js` builds exactly one `filterByFormula` and that it is a bare `prefixMatch` call, so a batch helper with a query of its own fails CI. Two queries for one rule is how the two drift.

**AN ISSUANCE HELPER NARROWER THAN A WRITER IS NOT AVAILABLE**, and it is worth knowing before someone tries. `mintDailyIds` calls its callback INSIDE the lock, because reading the highest sequence and writing the rows that claim it must be one critical section — a helper that returned ids would hand them out with the lock already released. So `createToolItems` mints and creates in one function, exactly as `createToolLogEntry` does one level down, and for the same reason.

**WHAT THE LOCK DOES NOT COVER** is `withKeyLock`'s standing residual: it serializes within one process or invocation only, so two concurrent Vercel invocations can still read the same highest sequence and both create. Every family has lived with that window; what differs here is the repair. No distributed lock is built — that is new machinery against a risk this base has never been observed to hit — and the frontend disable-on-click guard remains the other half. The module header says so where a reader of the code will meet it.

**A DELETED TOOL ITEM FREES ITS NUMBER IF IT WAS THE HIGHEST OF ITS DAY, WHICH IS THE ONE HAZARD ON THIS AXIS WORTH KNOWING.** `nextSequence` is MAX + 1 over the live rows, so deleting the top row lowers MAX and the next mint re-issues that number — measured, not reasoned: with `HYE-TL-260908-001..009` on the base, destroying `-009` and minting again produced `-009`. That is MAX + 1 working as specified, and for an invoice it is the accepted behavior #164 settled; here the number is on a sticker, so the same behavior would put one label on two tools with nothing to notice.

**WHAT CLOSES IT IS THE VOCABULARY, NOT THE GENERATOR.** The app offers no way to delete a tool item and must not: `Retired` exists so a tool can leave the count while its row and its whole log stay, which is the same argument that kept `Retired` when `In Repair` and `Lost` went. The only remaining exposure is a hand deletion in Airtable, which this base already forbids — nothing here is removed as tidying-up. A high-water mark in place of MAX + 1 would remove the hazard outright and is refused in `id-generation.md`, with the trade written down.

**NOTHING ROLLS BACK A PARTIAL REGISTRATION.** `createToolItems` returns what it created and what it did not, and a failed create simply stops the rest. Undoing the rows before it would free ids the counter has already spent, and a later registration would re-issue them; `nextSequence` is MAX + 1, so the gap costs nothing while a reused number costs two labels on two tools. What a short count leads to is #449's: the landing offers to write the rest.

**#313 HAS SINCE MOVED `Purchase Orders` ONTO THE TWO-DIGIT FORM, AND THIS FAMILY NEVER ADDED TO THAT WORK.** The tool item family took the two-digit form from the start. `offline/id-sequence.mjs` used to pin the split — five families on two digits, `PO` the one that is not — and that assertion went with the exception it described; what replaced it is that no family declares a year width at all, which is the same claim in the form it takes once there is nothing to split.

## The reverse links needed no disambiguation, and that is worth recording

Five link fields, five auto-created inverses, and all five are the name we would have chosen, so none was renamed: `Tools."Tool Items"`, `Jobs."Tool Items"`, `Tool Items."Tool Log"`, `Jobs."Tool Log"`, `Users."Tool Log"`.

Two links land on `Jobs`, which looks like the case `Users."PR Edit Requests (Initiated)"` and `"(Sent To)"` exist for. It is not. **Every disambiguated inverse on this base is two or more links from ONE source table** — `Purchase Orders (as PIC / as Manager / as Sender)`, `Jobs (as PIC / as Manager)`, the `PR Edit Requests` pair. Airtable names an inverse after the SOURCE TABLE, so two links from two different tables land already distinct.

## The ninth child relation, registered before anything writes a row

`"Tool Items::Tool Log"` is in `CHILD_KINDS` from the commit that creates the table, and `lib/airtable/toolLog.js:createToolLogEntry` is the `generateChildId` call site that makes it legal. Deferring the registration to #338 was proposed and reversed: the alternative was stating `{Tool Item ID}-{seq}` in the Airtable field description and having the next issue read it back out, which is one rule written twice in two places neither of which can check the other.

**`offline/id-sequence.mjs` requires a CALL SITE, not a writer with a screen behind it.** Its walker finds any `generateChildId(...)` under `lib/` and never asks whether anything calls that function in turn — measured by running it, 110 of 110 with the ninth relation registered and nothing but the credentialed check calling it.

**An issuance helper narrower than a writer is not available**, which is worth knowing before someone tries. `generateChildId` calls `createFn` INSIDE the per-parent lock, and that is the whole mechanism preventing two concurrent scans from minting one id; a helper that returned an id for the caller to use would hand it out with the lock already released. Minting and creating are one function by construction.

The relation shares its name from the moment it exists, which is the composite-key census confirming itself: `Tool Log` is carried by `Tool Items`, `Users` and `Jobs` the moment it exists, so a registry keyed on the bare field name would have had three candidates to be wrong about. See `id-generation.md`.

## The seven parent Record ID lookups deleted alongside (#334)

`PR Signers`, `PR Items`, `Quotations`, `PR Edit Requests` and `PR Edit Log` each carried a `PR Record ID` lookup, and `Invoice Items` carried `Invoice Record ID` and `PO Record ID`. All seven are gone; `Material Prices."Material Record ID"` and `"Vendor Record ID"` stay, are read by `getMaterialPrice`, and must not be swept with them.

**Nothing read the seven** — measured across `lib/`, `app/` and `scripts/`, the three names survive only in comments — and **no formula, rollup or lookup on the base referenced them**, measured by field id against the whole schema before the deletion. Each of the six tables had exactly one default `Grid view`.

**Deleting a lookup destroys nothing**, which is the fact that made this safe rather than merely tidy: a lookup stores no value, so recreating it with the same configuration restores the same cells. CLAUDE.md's warning that a type change can silently drop values is about a data field and does not reach this.

**The positive reason, which the `_Record ID` formulas do NOT share:** these seven existed only to support filtering a child table by its parent's record id, which this repository forbids — that lookup is computed asynchronously and undercounts siblings created right after the parent, reproduced for PO Items (`lib/ids.js:generateChildId`). Deleting them makes the banned query unwritable rather than merely forbidden. `PO Items."PO Record ID"` had additionally been MISCONFIGURED for as long as it existed (see `purchase-orders.md`), which is the same hazard one step further along.

**The six `_Record ID` formulas were examined and all six kept.** `Materials._Record ID` and `Vendors._Record ID` are load-bearing for the two `Material Prices` lookups. The other four — `Users`, `Purchase Requests`, `Purchase Orders`, `Invoices` — have no consumer at all now, and are kept anyway: they sit on the PARENT table so they enable nothing wrong, they are a human affordance for copying a record id out of the grid into a `verify-*` run, they cost nothing as formulas, and deleting four of six would leave an asymmetry the next reader has to re-derive.

`offline/table-field-names.mjs` gained the three names. They are **the first `RETIRED` entries with no successor field at all** — a third kind after a rename (#280, #333) and a replaced field (#318's `Paid` → `Paid Date`) — because what replaces the query is a method rather than a name: read the parent's reverse-link, which `getLinkedRecords` has always done.

---
